## G · Models

**(FOUNDER: *"we need to understand what models each agent gets because we don't need everyone running on Opus
or Fable or Astra or Terra ChatGPT models."*)** Every figure below is from models.md, fetched 2026-09-05.

### G.1 Model per agent per move

The default is the agent's row in §B.2. This table is what changes it, per move, and each rule names its trigger.

| Move | Model | Why this one |
|---|---|---|
| Any agent's default | as §B.2 — **five on `claude-opus-5`, eight on `claude-sonnet-5`, one split** | not everyone on the top tier, which is the founder's instruction |
| A build whose done-test has failed twice, horizon beyond one window | escalate to `claude-fable-5-1` | 1M context and **cache reads at 0.025x** against 0.1x everywhere else, so a large standing context is cheap to re-read. **Availability on a subscription seat is UNVERIFIED** — no plan table names Fable; fallback is `claude-opus-5` |
| A teammate inside an agent team | `claude-sonnet-5` | the vendor's own line: *"Use Sonnet for teammates"*, and teams use **~7x more tokens** when teammates run in plan mode |
| Routine scouting, mail sorting, link checks, the summarising half of the transcript pass | Gemini, once authenticated | it burns a different window and never touches the founder's |
| Embeddings, classification, dedup, PII detection | **local, on electricity** — MiniLM (384 dims, Apache 2.0, 256-word-piece truncation) and Qwen3-0.6B (32,768 context, Apache 2.0) | no window at all |
| A checker on a prepared diff | Codex `gpt-5.3-codex`, in the one position of §H | a second family, which is what the anchor ladder pays for |
| `/goal`'s evaluator and the auto-mode classifier | Haiku, **set by the vendor, not by us** | see G.4 |

**A blocking mechanism problem, and it must be fixed in the same change that writes an agent file:** this repo's
`scripts/prompt-standard.test.mjs` pins the valid model set to `claude-opus-5`, `claude-sonnet-5`,
`claude-fable-5`, `claude-haiku-4-5`. **`claude-fable-5-1` is not in it**, and `claude-fable-5` is now listed by
the vendor under *"Legacy models (still available)"*. An agent file written to G.1 fails a blocking lint today.

**(moved 2026-09-06: W2, W10, W17.)** **W2 sharpens the fix above into a rule:** write the **full id** in every
agent file and every argv, because *"`fable` and `best` … keep resolving to Fable 5 for now"* (world.md 2) —
an agent naming `fable` and one naming `claude-fable-5-1` are not the same routing, and the lint admission and
the first agent file move in one change. **W10:** an agent definition's `model:` now takes precedence over
`CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL`, which is what makes this whole table bind rather than describe — and it is also
what makes v59's strike of the teammate row (~~`claude-sonnet-5` for any teammate~~) actually take effect: a
teammate runs on its own file's model. **W17:** `PreModelSwitch` and `PostModelSwitch` are hook events that can
**block** a model switch (world.md 17). That is the gate this table has never had — it could enforce v57's
default or v21's escalation — and no row uses it, so it is named here and left to §12 rather than claimed.
Every rule in this table is still routing by declaration until §L **O5** generates the three copies of it from
`keel/shared/routing.yml` (contradiction 17).

**(amended 2026-09-06: E7 · v90.)** The Gemini and Codex rows are the second family **as CLIs run by `bin/run`
from launchd, never a key** (O92, W37); a cross-family verdict is rung 4 until the calibration set passes (v78);
same-family links ride `--fallback-model` (O89).

### G.2 The three windows and what each publishes

| Window | Published quota | Confidence |
|---|---|---|
| **Anthropic subscription** | **No numeric quota is published anywhere fetched.** Two windows exist — a rolling five-hour **and a weekly** — per seat, *"shared with Claude chat and Cowork"*. Magnitudes are relative only: Pro *"at least 5x more usage per 5-hour session than Free"*, Max *"5x"* / *"20x more usage than Pro"*. A Max window's real capacity is measurable only from an account. **The Max 20x price is UNVERIFIED** — the pricing page rendered *"From $100 per month"* for both Max tiers | H on the windows, and the absence of numbers is itself the finding |
| **OpenAI Codex** | **The only vendor publishing numeric per-window quotas.** Per 5-hour rolling window: GPT-6 Astra 5–45 (Plus) / 25–225 (Pro 5x) / 100–900 (Pro 20x); GPT-5.6 Sol 10–100 / 50–500 / 200–2,000; GPT-5.6 Luna 250–2,000 / 1,250–10,000 / 5,000–40,000. *"GPT-5.6 usage averages 5-30 credits per message"* | H. **Caveat:** `gpt-5.3-codex` appears in the API price list and in **no** plan quota row |
| **Gemini CLI** | ~~Free tier: **60 requests/min, 1,000 requests/day** with a personal Google account.~~ **THE PERSONAL-ACCOUNT TIER NO LONGER EXISTS (amended 2026-09-07: R40).** Google withdrew it on **2026-06-18** — *"This also applies to usage of Gemini CLI … you can no longer use the Login with Google option"* — and directs consumer accounts to the **Antigravity** family. **Standard and Enterprise subscriptions are explicitly unchanged.** Paid AI Pro / Ultra / Code Assist per-tier CLI quotas stay **UNVERIFIED** — not fetched. **CONFLICT, recorded not resolved:** the gemini-cli README still advertises the withdrawn sign-in and those exact free-tier numbers; two Google sources disagree, and the deprecation page is the more specific and the more recent | ~~H free~~ **H on the withdrawal, M on the conflict** (the README's freshness was not verified); unverified paid |

**Two limit shapes, and they behave differently.** A **seat** limit (*"session limit"*, *"weekly limit"*) is
shared across all models and **cannot be escaped with `/model`**. A **model-family** limit (*"your Opus limit"*)
can — switching family keeps the crew working. The Watch must distinguish them: one is a stop, the other is a
reroute.

### G.3 Cache facts that bind

- **Fable 5.1 and Mythos 5.1 read cache at 0.025x base input**; every other model is the standard 0.1x.
- **The write coefficient depends on the TTL you are buying:** 1.25x base input for a **5-minute** write, **2x
  for a 1-hour** write. FINAL §15.3's formula multiplies by 1.25 while assuming the one-hour TTL — **wrong
  coefficient for the TTL it assumes**, and its 0.10 sibling-read figure is **wrong by 4x for Fable**.
- **TTL is one hour on a subscription**, and drops to five minutes once usage credits are drawn, on an API key,
  or on a cloud provider. This is why standing prompts are byte-identical and carry no timestamp.
- **A tokenizer discontinuity crosses our own model list:** Opus 5 and Fable 5.x produce *"approximately 30%
  more tokens for the same text"* than Sonnet 4.6 and earlier. **Any token budget inherited from a
  Sonnet-4.6-era measurement understates by about that much.**
- **On a cache-dominated workload the model spread collapses, and this is the quantitative backing for v21.**
  List price runs 10x in and 10x out from Haiku 4.5 ($1/$5) to Fable 5.1 ($10/$50) — but Fable's cache reads
  ($0.25) are only **2.5x** Haiku's ($0.10). FINAL §14.5 measures context at 89% of the load, which is exactly
  the regime where the 0.025x read rate does the work. A long-horizon build with a large standing context is
  the one move where the top model is not priced like the top model.
- **Batch still needs a metered key** (FINAL §5.2 holds): 50% off both directions, and it **stacks with
  caching**. Batch prices are now published for every model, so the row is ready for the day a key exists.
- The one **vendor** cost anchor that exists is per developer-day, not per task: **$13 per active day, $150–250
  per month, under $30 per active day for 90% of users.** Every cost-per-task figure in circulation is
  third-party, confidence L, and is not used to route.
- **(moved 2026-09-06: W6.)** **TTL is a per-agent field now, not only an account property:**
  `experimental.cacheTtl` (`"5m"` or `"1h"`) is agent frontmatter *"used when no subagent TTL setting is
  configured"*, and `promptCacheTtl` / `subagentPromptCacheTtl` are settings that let the main conversation hold
  a one-hour cache while subagents stay at five minutes (world.md 6). **This is the row v57 rests on:** the
  0.025x read only pays on a warm cache, and warmth is now a declared field per agent — §B.2 carries the
  column. Pairs with §L **O39**, which hashes the standing prefix at dispatch, because a hit rate is a lagging
  indicator on a bill and a prefix hash is a leading indicator on a dispatch. **R7** measures the share.
- **(moved 2026-09-06: W1, W3, W4.)** **W1** confirms Fable 5.1's `$10/$50` with `$0.25/Mtok` cache reads.
  **W3:** Sonnet 5's `$2/$10` is *"its standard list price rather than a limited-time promo"* — any promo
  caveat on the Sonnet row is stale. **W4:** four numbers this section computes are now vendor-emitted
  structured fields — a per-session `prompt_cache` object (hit ratio, misses, tokens re-cached, warm/cold), a
  `rate_limits.spend_limit` status-line field, a per-loop `/usage` breakdown, and a `modelPricing` managed
  setting. **Read them rather than compute them**, and keep `keel/shared/prices.yml` (§L **O8**) for what they
  do not publish, with `fetched_at` and `valid_until` per row so a stale row refuses routing.

### G.4 Haiku 4.5's retirement

**Committed *"Not sooner than October 15, 2026"* — the nearest retirement date of any model this system names**,
and it is the only Haiku in the published table. Consequence, already taken as v20: **no agent's default is
Haiku.** It remains where the vendor sets it — `/goal`'s evaluator (changeable with
`ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_HAIKU_MODEL`, which changes it *everywhere the small fast model is used*) and the auto-mode
classifier. **Owner:** the founder, on a dated review; **mechanism:** the model ids carry an expiry in the facts
store and the store check fails a stale one.

### G.5 The terms question, stated once

*"Except when you are accessing our Services via an Anthropic API Key or where we otherwise explicitly permit
it, to access the Services through automated or non-human means, whether through a bot, script, or otherwise."*
The carve-out is an API key, and the escape hatch is *"where we otherwise explicitly permit it"* — which
Anthropic's own shipped features (`-p`, `--max-budget-usd`, `/loop`, Routines, Remote Control, agent teams)
exercise. ~~**Where a third-party program drives a subscription seat, that clause is the governing text and
nothing narrowing it was found.**~~ **THAT SENTENCE WAS FALSE and it is the reason this section had to be
re-read (amended 2026-09-07: review/terms-row-1.md, §A v108).** **The narrowing exists, it is quoted below,
and it narrows toward permission.** The clause above is verbatim correct and still current — it matches the
Consumer Terms character for character — but it is **one clause of five that bear on this**, and it is not
where the answer lives. ~~The no-metered-key choice and the automation question are therefore **one
question, not two**.~~ **They are one question and the answer is now recorded** — v90 for the key, **v108** for
the automation. **OpenAI's terms returned HTTP 403 and are unread. ~~Google's are unfetched.~~ ~~Google's are unfetched, and since
E7 (v90) routes the second family through the Gemini CLI on a personal account, that reading now gates a live
route — R40 (amended 2026-09-06).~~ Google's terms are READ (2026-09-07: R40) and forbid nothing relevant —
policies.google.com/terms restricts automated access only *"in violation of the machine-readable instructions
on our web pages"*, conditional on robots.txt, and ai.google.dev/gemini-api/terms carries no clause against
scripted use. That reading no longer gates a live route, because R40 also found the route itself withdrawn
(v90).** ~~It is a founder decision after one reading, and it is §I row 1.~~ **§I row 1 is CLOSED by v108.**

**The document that spends the carve-out, which this section did not cite** — Claude Code legal and
compliance, <https://code.claude.com/docs/en/legal-and-compliance>, accessed 2026-09-07. It names which terms
govern (*"Consumer Terms of Service - for Free, Pro, and Max users"*), and it carries **the permission**:
*"OAuth authentication is intended exclusively for purchasers of Claude Free, Pro, Max, Team, and Enterprise
subscription plans and is designed to support ordinary use of Claude Code and other native Anthropic
applications."* It carries **the prohibition, whose subject is the load-bearing part**: *"Anthropic does not
permit third-party developers to offer Claude.ai login into their own applications, or to route requests
through Free, Pro, or Max plan credentials on behalf of their users. Moreover, developers may not collect,
store, or intermediate Claude.ai credentials or session tokens."* And it carries **the narrowing of that
prohibition**: *"Nor does it prevent an end user from signing in to the unmodified Claude Code binary with
their own Claude subscription."* It carries **the volume qualifier**: *"Advertised usage limits for Pro and
Max plans assume ordinary, individual usage of Claude Code and the Agent SDK."* It carries **the enforcement
sentence**: *"Anthropic reserves the right to take measures to enforce these restrictions and may do so
without prior notice."* And it carries **the prohibition Keel could most plausibly trip**: *"The Claude Code
binary must not be modified. Claude Code must be installed and run as published by Anthropic, and customers
may not remove, disable, or restrict any authentication method built into it."* — mechanism **O129**.

**This page shows no last-updated date.** That is worth stating about the document now carrying the operative
language: its content can be dated only to an access, not to a publication. The Consumer Terms still show an
October 2025 effective date, so **this is the page to re-read when the terms are revisited, not the Consumer
Terms.**

**The most consequential sentence in the whole terms answer, and it is an absence: not one Anthropic document
distinguishes an attended run from an unattended one, or mentions night, sleep, or the absence of a human.
The attended/unattended axis this plan is organised around does not appear in Anthropic's text.** The axis
Anthropic draws sharply is **whose usage it is** — first-party versus third-party — and by that axis this
harness sits on the permitted side, with the undefined volume qualifier as the live question rather than the
automation. Read that as scoping the *terms* argument and nothing else: attended/unattended remains the right
axis for **risk**, for the cord, for `night_capable` and for what a run may do with nobody watching. It is
simply not the axis the licence is written on, and every §A row that argued the terms from it was arguing from
a distinction the source does not draw.

---

## H · Codex and Claude Code from day one

**(FOUNDER, overruling FINAL row 30: *"I run from day one of the system to include codex and Claude code in the
system."*)**

### H.1 The shape, decided

**A third program drives both.** The Operator is the founder's contact point and runs inside Claude Code; every
dispatch, to any provider, goes through one no-model launcher that emits that provider's argv.

| Option | Verdict | Why |
|---|---|---|
| Claude Code drives Codex from Bash | **rejected as the primary shape** | it is exactly the shape #19945 breaks — no controlling TTY plus a non-trivial prompt — and the `script -qfc` cure is *"incompatible with normal background / parallel job execution"*, which is what a crew is |
| Codex drives Claude | **rejected** | no documented mechanism in either direction; Codex is not installed |
| **A third program drives both** | **CHOSEN** | each vendor documents a headless invocation, a session id, resume, an instructions file, a SKILL.md bundle and MCP. A provider outage or a defect becomes a routing change, not a rewrite. It is also the only shape that keeps v34 true — one thing composes argv |
| Hybrid | folded in | the Floor is interactive Claude Code and always was; the founder may run Codex by hand there. That is the hybrid, and it needs no mechanism |

### H.2 Codex's position on day one, and the test that widens it

**Day one:** `codex exec` as a **checker on a prepared diff**, run in the foreground with stdout redirected to a
file while inheriting the parent shell's TTY — the second documented workaround, which does not need
`script -qfc`. **The cost, stated once: one foreground slot is not parallel, so Codex is not a night lane yet.**

**The admission test that widens it** — and it is specifiable from primary text now, which it was not when FINAL
was written: `codex exec --json`, **no controlling TTY**, a **non-trivial prompt**, on a version **≥ 0.124.0**,
against known-answer cases. Pass and Codex becomes a night checker and a maker on mid-hard work; fail and it
stays in the foreground slot. **#19945 has been open 130 days with no maintainer reply**, so the test is the
plan, not the issue closing.

### H.3 Where `/goal` and `/loop` sit

- **`/goal` is on the run, and the done-test is the goal condition** (v12). `claude -p "/goal <done-test> or
  stop after N turns"`; `--output-format stream-json --verbose` emits each message as the loop runs; the
  condition limit is 4,000 characters; the three verdicts are Not yet met, Met, Impossible. It terminates on
  Met, Impossible, `/goal clear`, or four unrecoverable errors, and **leaves the goal active after transient
  failures including rate limits** — which is the behaviour a night wants.
- **A goal defers evaluation while a subagent or background shell is running**; check-ins start at 30 minutes
  and double to a 4x ceiling, and under `-p` **check-ins are the only way the runtime delivers anything**.
- **`/loop` is refused in production** and stays a Floor convenience: session-scoped, 7-day expiry, and it fires
  *"only while Claude Code is running and idle"*. The Watch is the loop.
- **Codex `/goal` is not used.** It exists (0.128.0, 2026-04-30) with states including `budget_limited`, but
  **whether it runs under `codex exec` is not established**, and its own tracker carries an open issue that a
  budget-limited goal cannot resume. Revisit when H.2's test passes.

### H.4 The managed-settings collision, resolved

Written once in v11 and repeated here because it is where a section-writer will trip: the managed settings file
gives the grant a tier a running process cannot clear, **and setting `disableAllHooks` or `allowManagedHooksOnly`
in it kills `/goal`**, because `/goal` is a session-scoped prompt-based Stop hook. The file therefore carries
deny rules~~, `disableBypassPermissionsMode` and `disableAutoMode`~~ and `disableBypassPermissionsMode` only
(amended 2026-09-06: E9 · v92), and **not** the two hook settings. Narrowing is
carried by `--restricted` plus an explicit `--tools` list, which does not touch hooks.

### H.5 What moved under this section on 2026-09-06 (W19–W25, W27)

Nothing above is deleted. Six facts and one absence land here, and one of them is a whole missing row.

- **W19 · the rehearsal floor no longer discriminates.** Codex is at **0.153.4 (2026-09-04)**; H.2's
  ~~*"version ≥ 0.124.0"*~~ is 29 minor versions stale and is satisfied by anything installed. Restate it as
  **the installed version, recorded** (v32, and §L **O35** records a binary's version string and sha256 at
  admission).
- **W24 · the finding on the two questions this section most needs answered is *absence*.** Nothing in the
  Codex entries readable for 2026-08-26 → 2026-09-04 mentions `codex cloud exec`, a cloud API, cancel, poll,
  goal mode or **#19945**. **H.2 stands and v56(b) stands.** Absence is not denial; the June–August window is
  unread after three fetch failures, and reading it is **R26** — the only cheap route to **R10**, the hinge.
  One correction inward: 0.152.0 adds credential-refresh progress to `codex exec`, **adding output to the
  stream H.2 depends on being clean**.
- **W21 · Codex has a third control axis, Guardian** — see §C.1. The two-axis cell in the band table
  understates a Codex run, and Codex subagents keep their own review history.
- **W22 · Codex shipped `Interrupt` hooks** — *"run commands or MCP handlers when an active top-level turn is
  interrupted"*. **That is the cord's natural Codex-side receiver** (v67), and no row named it before this one.
- **W20 · Codex ships `@` mentions between tasks**, so agents can *"read, create, or message tasks from
  terminal"* — the second provider to ship agent-to-agent messaging in one window. Shape decided in **v80**.
- **W23 · a Codex extension can *"inspect or replace MCP tool results before reaching the model"*.** An
  admitted tool's **output** can be rewritten before the model sees it, which is a taint path §F's door does
  not test for. Pairs with §L **O65**'s taint id and canary string.
- **W25, W27 · §H has no Gemini row of any kind, and that is now a hole with a name.** Gemini CLI ships named
  subagents with their own tools, MCP servers and context windows, delegated by `@agent` and defined in
  `~/.gemini/agents` or `.gemini/agents` — **a third agent-file location beside `.claude/agents/` and Codex
  TOML**, which v42 did not contemplate. Its releases since 0.30 are dominated by security hardening, including
  *"enforce fail-closed workspace trust and filter mcpServers in restricted mode"* — **the nearest analogue to
  `--restricted` in any third runtime**, and the thing a Gemini row would be written against.

| 19 | **The sandbox exemption `codex exec` needs** — `denyRead` on `~/.codex`, plus provider TLS at the egress proxy | without it a Codex resolver **cannot run from inside a Claude session at all**: sandboxed, `codex exec` is **exit 1 with 0 bytes**, dying on *"Failed to read config file /Users/adamks/.codex/config.toml: Operation not permitted"* before it reaches the network. Every one of the 20 working invocations required `dangerouslyDisableSandbox` | `~/.codex` is on **O92**'s deny list **on purpose** — it holds another provider's credentials, and the point of `denyRead` is that a persuaded agent cannot read them. The sandbox exposes no narrow per-invocation allow for this; the only lever is the all-or-nothing escape hatch, which is a guardrail against accident and not containment. **And the exemption may not be needed for the design as written:** O92 already routes Codex as a `bin/run` child **from launchd, never from a Claude-hosted shell**, which is outside the Bash sandbox entirely — so this row governs a resolver dispatched **inside a session**, and nothing else | the founder — it is a policy call on a credential deny rule, not an agent's | **OPEN — raised 2026-09-07 by the headless rehearsal (review/codex-rehearsal.md), and deliberately not decided there or here.** **Two blockers, either sufficient, and they are NOT separated by the evidence:** sandboxed `codex doctor` gets further and shows *"ChatGPT inference URL … TLS handshake or certificate validation failed (required)"* at the egress proxy; unsandboxed the same command is `19 ok · 0 fail`. Both were removed together by disabling the sandbox, so **whether lifting `denyRead` alone would suffice is untested**. **The discrimination rule this row exists to protect (Rule 10):** `exit 0 + 0 bytes` is #19945; **`exit 1 + 0 bytes` is our sandbox**; a resolver that keys on *empty stdout* reports one `unresolved` for a broken tool and a denied one |

## J · Losing-images ledger — additions

Kept by name so each can be argued for later. Five are the founder's overrules; the rest are moved by research, 25–30 by the orchestrator's build-round decisions (DECISIONS.md §8–§10), 31–42 by the review round, 43–54 by the founder's interview (DECISIONS §15), **55–72 by the rethink round of 2026-09-06**, and **73–88 by the fixer round (DECISIONS §24)** — 73–75 the founder's overrules, 76–88 the lane shapes the fold did not take, each with `source:` and `wins_if:` — **89–90 added 2026-09-06 by the orchestrator (v96 and v97 had no §J entry), 91 by the surfaces round (v107), and 92 by the research close (v108).** **(Amended 2026-09-07: this preamble stopped at 88 while three entries had already landed past it, and 89–91 were sitting AFTER the `---` that closes this section, outside their own section body. Both are fixed here.)** **Do not quote the count from this sentence; derive it:** `awk '/^## J ·/,/^## K ·/' SPINE.md \| grep -oE '^[0-9]+(–[0-9]+)?\.' \| tr -d '.' \| tr '–' '\n' \| sort -n \| tail -1` → the highest entry id, **92**, which is also the entry count, because 31–42 is one line carrying twelve.

that would make the losing image the better answer. Entries **1–54 are untouched here on purpose**: they get
their `wins_if:` in §22 from the builders, written by the people who wrote the reasons. An entry with no
`wins_if:` is a graveyard plot; an entry with one is a live alternative waiting for a fact.

## K · The section plan for the builders

Twenty-five sections (13a added in the build round). Each names the SPINE rows it must obey and the FINAL sections it inherits. **A section may
not decide anything not in §A; if it needs a decision that is not there, it returns BLOCKED to the Operator.**

**The fifth column is the rethink round's brief, by file.** It names the D rows (§A v66–v82), the O ids (§L), the
W ids (§M) and the R ids (§N) that touch each section, plus **the contradictions of SYNTHESIS §5 named in the
section that carries the wrong sentence** — a contradiction spanning three sections is named in all three,
because the section that must change is the one holding the sentence, not the one holding the fix.

**The sixth column is the fixer round's brief, by section (DECISIONS §24)** — the rows that land in each, so a
builder is dispatched by section. **No section may reintroduce** a box, a key or a vanilla week (§J 73–75).

**The surfaces round of 2026-09-07 adds no seventh column for one row.** Its additions sit at the end of the sixth
column carrying their own date — *(2026-09-07: v107)* — so a builder can tell a fixer-round instruction from a
surfaces-round one without a column that would be empty in twenty-four of twenty-five rows.

**Discipline every section carries:** provenance on every paragraph (FOUNDER · FINAL · NEW: why) · every rule
names its mechanism or is marked WISH · every path exists on a named branch or is marked ABSENT · no stage
states method · no schedule and no durations · the founder's words quoted wherever they decide something ·
anything not in the inputs marked UNVERIFIED.

---

## L · Mechanisms decided by the orchestrator in the rethink round (O1–O80), the fixer round (O81–O127) and the research close (O128–O130)

**These are not founder decisions and they are not §A rows.** Each follows from a rule already on this page, a
measured fact, or a contradiction inside v2 — the class DECISIONS §10 and §12 settled without the founder.
Copied from rethink/SYNTHESIS.md §2.1–2.3 and **not re-argued here**: the reason each is a fix rather than a
question lives there.

**Status has three values.** **ADOPTED** — it follows from a rule on the page or a fact, and a builder may
proceed. **ADOPTED-AS-SPEC** — the decision is taken and the *act* is deferred; one entry, O38, because the
founder deferred the hook rewrite to build time (DECISIONS §15). **DEPENDS-ON-R** — a named research answer
decides whether or in what shape the thing exists; the R is named and §N carries it. Where an R sharpens a fix
without gating it, the R is named in the sentence and the status stays ADOPTED. **`Path`** follows v50: ABSENT
means designed and named but not built; WISH means no mechanism is designed; anything else names what exists on
this branch today.

**Two columns on every row since 2026-09-06 (v96, O105).** `class` is `kernel` (direction · record · truth ·
taste), `adapter` (naming the vendor surface) or `refuse`; `vendor_wins_if:` is the surface whose arrival forces
Delete (`—` where none is conceivable). O1–O80: C-strategist §2 C3's sort where it names the row **(C)**, else
**(NEW)**. **L.4** (O81–O127) carries `wins_if:` too, and so does **L.5** (O128–O130).

**A `DEPENDS-ON-R` whose R has been answered does not become `ADOPTED` by that fact alone.** The research
close of 2026-09-07 answered four of them and each resolved differently: **R22** made O40 conditional on a
different, nameable thing rather than unblocking it; **R25** disqualified O52's candidate carrier and named
the replacement; **R21** decided O32's open half; **R14 stays open**, so O41 and O114 do not move. Read each
row's own status, and note that an answered R can leave a mechanism *more* blocked than it found it.

### L.1 Stores and schemas the one-writer rule already implies (O1–O12)

### L.2 Programs the design needs and does not name (O13–O20, O78, O79)

| id | The fix | Path | Moves | Status | class | vendor_wins_if |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **O13** | **A local-tier carrier.** The local tier has **no reachable one**: the armed sandbox denies a loopback `bind()` and its consumer `curator` carries no `Bash` and no MCP. Make it no-model programs in the Watch's launchd context, handing the curator a file — v47's shape | `bin/embed` · `bin/classify` — ABSENT | §15 · §13 | **ANSWERED (R4) — ~~DEPENDS-ON-R4~~; ADOPTED, and the answer is the one this row assumed** (2026-09-07). R4 measured that a sandboxed child reaches the live Ollama through **neither Bash nor WebFetch**, and that inbound `bind()` is denied for unix sockets as well as TCP — ~~**so the local tier is not a service in either direction**~~, and no-model programs in the launchd context is the shape that survives rather than the shape that was preferred. **REVERSED IN PART 2026-09-07 (W47): `sandbox.network.allowLocalBinding: true` ALONE reached the live Ollama on `127.0.0.1:11434` and returned `200`, and bound at three process depths — so BOTH directions open on one key and the local tier CAN be a service.** **The row's SHAPE is not reopened here and its carrier does not change:** `bin/embed`/`bin/classify` in the launchd context needs no key at all, and the founder's *“change nothing yet”* means the key is not set. What changed is the **reason** — it was *unreachable*, and it is now merely *unset* — and W47 carries an unreconciled open vendor issue reporting the opposite, so this is a reversal recorded rather than a decision taken | adapter · `ollama` from the launchd context (C; W36 confirms programs) | ~~the sandbox admits a loopback connect per invocation (R4)~~ **TESTED 2026-09-07 AND NOT MET — outbound loopback is denied on two independent tool channels. The one shape still untested is an MCP stdio server**, spawned by the CLI as a child of its own process rather than of the Bash sandbox; if that reaches 127.0.0.1 the local tier can be an MCP tool and this row's carrier changes. One cell settles it, and it means writing a server. **AND THE CONDITION WAS MET 2026-09-07 BY A DIFFERENT ROUTE (W47) — not per invocation but per settings key:** `allowLocalBinding: true` admits exactly the loopback `connect()` the strike above says was tested and denied. **Both readings stand on this row on purpose.** The MCP-stdio shape named here is still untested and is no longer the only route open |
| **O14** | **A worktree program.** v41 gives four agents `isolation: worktree`, `git worktree add` cannot complete under the armed sandbox, and interactive escalation is unavailable to an unattended run by construction. **A run never creates its own and is handed one in its argv** | `bin/worktree` — ABSENT | §6 · §15 | ADOPTED | adapter · `-w/--worktree` (C) | `-w` completes under the armed sandbox without escalation |

| **O16** | **The Watch/Sender lease.** A lease file holds a host id and a heartbeat; the Watch refuses to tick without it and the Sender refuses to act without it, so the restore drill's clone can never tick and never send. **The only failure in the round that ends in a duplicated outward act** | `logbook/watch.lease` — ABSENT | §15 | ADOPTED | kernel · truth (NEW) | — |

### L.4 The fixer round's mechanisms, three lanes merged into one numbering (O81–O127)

Decided under DECISIONS §24, never from a lane the founder overruled. Status names the E-row and the lane(s);
one row per mechanism however many lanes designed it.

| **O81** | `night_capable` from `pmset` ~~(AC · `sleep 0`/`disablesleep 1` · assertions)~~ every tick; an `unattended` brief refused when false and routed to the cloud carrier where `cloud: allow`; a page-3 fact; the probe sleeps a child. **(moved 2026-09-07: R37 + R41 — the contract is now measured, and both halves of the old one were insufficient.)** **Three conditions, in this order: AC present AND charge above a floor AND the assertion binding.** **(0) A PROHIBITION BEFORE THE THREE CONDITIONS, added 2026-09-07 in correction round 1 and load-bearing: the predicate may NOT be built on sleep-episode length, and *"no long sleep episodes"* is not evidence of anything.** Power Nap's 2-second DarkWake every ~16 minutes terminates an episode, so **a predicate reading episodes would have passed EVERY NIGHT of 2026-08-31 → 09-07, including the eleven-hour one** (research/close-C.md). Availability on this machine is the **contiguous span between full `Wake` events**, and that is the only unit `power.yml` may record. **(1) Charge is first and it is new:** R37's 6 h 41 min gap was `'Low Power Sleep'` at **1% charge** — a state **no assertion of any kind prevents** — and a predicate built from sleep settings and assertions alone would have returned **true through the entire window the machine was dark**. *(That gap is now understood as the one episode Power Nap did not break; the condition it justifies is unchanged and its reason is sharper.)* **(2) The assertion is LAST, and reading it takes both halves of `pmset -g assertions`:** the per-process listing to know the assertion is ours (`pid <N>(caffeinate): … PreventUserIdleSystemSleep named: "caffeinate command-line tool"`, appearing within 2 s and gone on exit) **and** the system-wide counter to know it binds. Each alone is wrong in its own direction — **the counter alone reads 1 at baseline with no caffeinate running** (held by `coreaudiod` and by `powerd`'s *"Prevent sleep while display is on"*), and **the listing alone says true on battery where nothing binds**: measured, `caffeinate -s` was listed per-process while system-wide `PreventSystemSleep` stayed **0**. **Treat listed-but-zero as FALSE.** **(3) A bounded `caffeinate` does NOT substitute for `pmset -a disablesleep 1`** — `-i` is idle-only by the vendor's own one-line definition, `-s` is *"valid only when system is running on AC power"*, **the man page names no flag that speaks to the lid**, and two `Clamshell Sleep`s were measured **inside** a held `PreventSystemSleep` window (both on battery, consistent with the AC-only restriction). **The AC lid close is unmeasured** — no AC clamshell sleep in the retained log falls inside a held window — and it is **BLOCKED on a physical act by the founder**, not on a lane | `bin/watch` · `keel/host/power.yml` · `bin/probe` — ABSENT | §4 · §15 · §14 | ADOPTED (E13; A) — **R37 (re-specified), R41** | adapter · `pmset` | ~~a night with the predicate true all night ends `orphaned`~~ **(2026-09-07) that `wins_if:` is UNFALSIFIABLE under the old unit and is restated: a night with the predicate true all night ends `orphaned`, WITH the night's contiguous-span reading attached** — under episode counting the predicate is true on an eleven-hour absence, so the old form could fire on a machine that was simply dark and teach the wrong lesson | the runtime refuses unattended work on a sleeping host |

### L.5 The research close's mechanisms (O128–O130)

Three rows, 2026-09-07. **O128 is not new work** — it is v107's mechanism, moved out of its §A row and into this
section where every other mechanism in this plan lives. The previous lane wrote it inside the row because its
brief named §K, §M and §N and not §L, and flagged the call rather than taking it. **O129 and O130 are new**:
one is the hard rule v108 adds, the other is the disposition COVERAGE was waiting for.

| id | The fix | Path · state | Moves | Status | class | wins_if | vendor_wins_if |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **O128** | **The attention discipline's mechanism** (v107, moved here 2026-09-07): `keel/surfaces/styles/<class>.md`, **one body per surface class** — briefing · which · card · rendered-record — plus a **`style:` key on O12's page manifest**. The marks lint (O105) **refuses a founder-read surface carrying no `style:` and refuses a machine-read payload that declares one**, and that second refusal is the only mechanism standing between v107 and the damage it could do. **Keel writes its own bodies** — rules are not copyrightable, the prose expressing them is; the upstream plugin is adopted only for the founder's own Floor sessions, where using it creates no derivative. **The row's text is unchanged and now points here** | `keel/surfaces/styles/<class>.md` · `keel/surfaces/pages/<n>.yml` · the marks lint — **ABSENT** | §14 · §3 · §4 · §21 · §17 · §19 | ADOPTED (v107) — **moved, not re-decided**; nothing about it changes by living here | **both halves, and the split is v107's**: `kernel · taste` for *what the founder must be able to see in one line*, `adapter · outputStyle` for *rendering a response in a style*. **O105's lint must accept a row that carries both**, or it forces a false single answer | v107's — over a month under O95's meter, briefing lines acted on **without opening the artifact behind them**, beside founder-minutes per finished intent; it **loses on one founder act reversed for an omission** | for the adapter half: the runtime ships per-surface, per-invocation style selection our renderer would only be wrapping. For the kernel half: **—**, none is conceivable, since no vendor decides what this founder must see |
| **O129** | **The Claude Code binary is never modified** (v108's hard rule, and the explicit prohibition Keel could most plausibly trip): *"Claude Code must be installed and run as published by Anthropic, and customers may not remove, disable, or restrict any authentication method built into it."* **`bin/run` composes argv AROUND the published binary and may not patch, repackage, shim or wrap-by-replacement it, and may not pass anything that removes, disables or restricts a built-in authentication method.** A launcher that **wraps** `claude` is fine; one that **patches** it is not, and the line between the two is: does the bytes-on-disk hash of the binary the child executes match the published install. **`bin/probe` records the runtime binary's version string and sha256 and refuses to mint when it differs** — this is **O35's idiom extended from MCP servers and CLIs to the runtime itself**, which O35 already noted was *"undefined for the binaries that actually run at night"* | `bin/run` · `bin/probe` · the admitted-binary record (O35) — **ABSENT**. **Be honest about the lint: there is none today, and none is named.** The nearest existing thing is O35's admission hash, which is also ABSENT; nothing in this repo currently checks that a launcher has not patched its runtime, so this is `ABSENT`, never `EXISTS` | §12 · §15 · §8 | ADOPTED (v108) | kernel · truth | a year in which no launcher change ever came near the binary — the hash check then cost one probe step and bought nothing | the runtime verifies its own integrity and refuses to run patched |
| **O130** | **Voice output gets a mechanism, which is what it has never had.** COVERAGE carries `Voice output tool` (*the briefing read aloud*) and `Audio playback tool` (*the raw work played, not a summary of it*) **both `IN`, both §10, both against v2 §14, with no mechanism anywhere** — not among §L's rows, not among the `bin/` programs, not on any of §D's seven pages, and `grep -rn 'read aloud\|text-to-speech\|TTS\|playback' SPINE.md parts/` returns nothing about either. Voice **input** is placed (v29 refuses it as binding, v38 keeps the read-back a published phone page for it); voice **output** was placed nowhere. **The fix: `bin/say`, one no-model program over two binaries macOS already ships** — `say` for text, `afplay` for an artifact — which speaks **only** text a program has already rendered (the briefing O95 renders, a `Decide` item's own line) and plays **only** an artifact a log row already names, and **refuses any string not already on a surface**. **It writes nothing, decides nothing and binds nothing** — v29 stands untouched in both directions, and **v107/O128 bind the source text, never the rendering**. **It is not the bell:** O18 stays the only thing that may **ring**; this is a **reading**, and a program that reads is not a program that interrupts | `bin/say` — **ABSENT** | §14 · §17 · §23 (COVERAGE §10) | ADOPTED 2026-09-07 — **placed, not solved.** COVERAGE left both rows `IN` and unamended on purpose, saying the disposition was the plan's to take; **this is the plan taking it.** **The alternative was REFUSED-with-a-reason and it was not taken**, because the founder asked for it twice and the cheapest honest form is one no-model program over binaries already on the machine. **Leaving it `IN` and unmechanised was the one option the doctrine forbids** | adapter · macOS `say` / `afplay` | a quarter in which the founder never plays one — the briefing is read on a screen and this is a memory of an ask, not a surface | the runtime speaks a rendered surface itself |

### L.6 The sandbox panel's mechanism (O131)

**One row, 2026-09-07, and it is the whole design consequence of §M W50 and W51.** Both measured ways a child
ends up unconfined while its own settings still read `"enabled": true` travel through **a settings file
someone else supplies**. Everything else the panel found is a fact; this is the one finding that has to become
a program — and the program is one this plan already names and has not built. **It is a mechanism, not a
posture:** it says what the dispatcher must *do* when it exists, and takes no position on what its template
should *contain*, which is §I row 19's and the founder's.

| id | The fix | Path · state | Moves | Status | class | wins_if | vendor_wins_if |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **O131** | **The dispatcher builds each child's settings from a FIXED TEMPLATE IT OWNS, and never accepts a settings file as a parameter.** Any brief, playbook, tool result or fetched text that can choose the `--settings` file handed to `claude -p` has thereby chosen that child's confinement: `filesystem.disabled: true` switches the filesystem half off from the command line (W51), and `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` usually skips initialisation outright (W50). The template belongs to the dispatcher; **the run supplies values INSIDE it** — cwd, the worktree's `denyWrite` list (W45), the run id — **and never the file itself**. The same rule covers argv: the permission mode is the template's, not the brief's | `bin/run` — the child-settings writer · **ABSENT**. `keel/` does not exist on this branch, so this mechanism has no owner yet, and **that absence is also the deferral's trigger: the sandbox posture is revisited when `bin/run` exists**, because that is the first moment a per-child settings file could be written by anything this plan owns | **W50 · W51 · W52 · W57 · W58 · O92 · O93 · §C.4** | **ADOPTED as a mechanism 2026-09-07 · NO POSTURE.** Its cost is measured at **none**: it prevents per-run settings flexibility, and nothing in this plan currently needs any. It is also **not sufficient by itself** — W52 says the strongest control in a session is one nothing here configures, and R46 is what measures whether a `-p` child has it at all | program · the dispatcher's own template; no vendor key is involved and none would help | a dispatcher that never gains a caller-supplied settings path at all — if `bin/run` is the only writer and no brief, playbook or tool result can name a `--settings` file, this row is a description of what already happens rather than a rule anything must enforce | the vendor makes either path **report** — a child that skipped sandbox initialisation, or that had `filesystem.disabled` set through `--settings`, says so somewhere a caller can read. **Today nothing anywhere reports either** (W50, W51), and that silence is what makes this mechanism ours |

one**, and the two paragraphs under it already said so — the header simply never moved. **W1–W32** are

on this Mac (rethink-2/A-practitioner.md §6), several of them re-measured by census D the same day. **W42** is
the founder's own pointer plus a second-hand summary, REPORTED and not fetched. **A source class per row is
the point of this table**, so a header claiming one source for all of them was the one sentence here that
could not be checked against its own contents. **Each names the exact row and
the exact change, and each has been applied in place** — the moved rows carry a bracketed
*(moved 2026-09-06: W-n)* note and nothing was deleted to make room for one. Where a W falsified a clause, the
clause is struck and the new sentence sits beside it.

**W33–W41 (fixer round) are not world.md's** but thinker A's measurements on this Mac (A-practitioner.md §6),
carried as (THINKER: An), bound for `facts.yml` (O117).

**W42 (surfaces round, 2026-09-07) is neither, and its source class is the weakest in this table.** It is the
founder's own pointer plus a second-hand summary, and it is **REPORTED, not fetched**: the session that wrote it
had no `WebFetch` and no `WebSearch`, so **every clause of it about the upstream repository is second-hand**. It
therefore carries the exact fetches that settle it instead of an access date it cannot honestly claim. It moves
**v107** only in the sense of naming what v107 is answering; **no number in it moves anything**, which is the
whole reason it is admissible in this state.

**W43–W46 (correction round 2, 2026-09-07) are a fourth source class and the strongest in this table: measured
on this Mac by the `reviewer` engine that spawned the children** (research/close-C.md), each with a stated
cell count and, where it matters, a positive control. **W43 and W44 were not asked for by any §N question** —
they cut across the lane and bear on the build plan rather than on one row, which is what this table is for.
**W45 and W46 sit beneath an answered question (R1, R32) and are lifted here because each falsifies something
this repository or this plan currently does**, and a fact buried inside an answer is a fact nobody re-reads.
**Single family, one agent, one pass, one machine, one day** — and W43's remedy clause is documentary, from
the sandbox panel's vendor read rather than from a cell.

**W47–W58 (the sandbox panel, 2026-09-07) are a fifth source class, and it is mixed on purpose: five sealed
lanes, three of which measured and two of which read.** P1 ran **37 `claude -p` children across three rounds**
and every cell below it carries its own negative controls; P2 ran six adversarial probes inside a live session;
P3 walked one designed night end to end against the files as they stand; P4 read the vendor's documentation
and tracker and **had no shell**; P5 read this repository's own configuration. **Every row is a FACT AND AN
OPEN CONSEQUENCE and nothing more. The founder decided *"change nothing yet"* on the sandbox posture
(DECISIONS.md §29, G2), so no row here takes a posture, none amends §I row 19, none writes a recommendation
and none edits a settings file.** The deferral's trigger is named rather than dated: **revisit when `bin/run`
exists**, because that is the first moment a per-child settings file could be written by anything this plan
owns.

**Two of these rows are weaker than their headline and are written at the weaker strength on purpose.** W50's
effect is real and large and its mechanism is **not** deterministic — 8 of 9, with one cell unexplained — and a
row that wrote it as *skips* would be worse than no row at all. W47 **could not be reconciled** with an open
vendor issue that reports the opposite result, and the lane that measured it **did not try**; that
irreconcilable half travels inside the row rather than being resolved into a theory that would read cleanly
and rest on nothing.

| **W33** | `pmset -g custom` → `sleep 1` on AC and battery; **391 maintenance sleeps, 74 back-to-sleep, 24 clamshell in seven days** (THINKER: A1) — and **the `pmset -g log` window is rolling and moves hourly, so these counts are a reading and not a constant** — 391/74 at one reading on 2026-09-06 and 348/77 at the next, with the span sliding 156 h → 168 h (census D). **The clamshell count of 24 was exact at both readings, and the finding that survives every reading is what the row rests on: ~~zero episodes of an hour or more~~ (struck 2026-09-07: R37).** ~~**It survived every reading because every reading was of the same seven-day window.**~~ **(amended again 2026-09-07, correction round 1.) IT SURVIVED EVERY READING BECAUSE IT IS INVARIANT TO THE THING IT WAS BEING USED TO MEASURE — and invariance was read here as robustness, which is the trap.** Power Nap raises a 2-second DarkWake about every **16 minutes**, each one **terminates a sleep episode** in the log's own accounting, and therefore **no episode can reach an hour on this machine while Power Nap is on.** *Zero episodes of an hour or more* was not a finding about this Mac's availability; it was a property of the metric. **Counting contiguous spans between full `Wake` events over the SAME week gives 29 spans, 70.0 h, longest 11.08 h, fourteen of an hour or more.** ~~`pmset -g log` retains ~7 days; `/var/log/powermanagement` retains ~15 and `pmset` does not show it — **W33, R5 and census D all read only the shorter one, three readings sharing one blind spot.**~~ **The window length was a real second defect and a much smaller one; the unit is the defect that mattered.** ~~**The surviving finding now reads: *zero episodes of an hour or more while on AC; one of 6 h 41 min on a flat battery in fourteen days*** — 2026-08-28 00:48:20Z → 07:28:56Z, cause `'Low Power Sleep'`, waking on AC at 1% charge, with the second-longest episode in the whole 14.40-day window at **18.0 minutes**.~~ **NOTHING OF THIS ROW'S ORIGINAL FINDING SURVIVES AS A STATEMENT ABOUT AVAILABILITY. What survives is a statement about the log: 391/74/24 and *zero episodes ≥ 1 h* are correct readings of the episode metric, and the episode metric cannot answer *how long is this machine away*.** The 6 h 41 min episode is the exception that proves the mechanism — `TCPKeepAlive=inactive` on a flat battery, i.e. **Power Nap off, so nothing broke the episode**. **R37** — thirty days including a weekend away, as a standing intent (O82) — is still what settles it, and **it is now PARTIAL at 14.40 of 30 days with the other sixteen already rotated out of the store**; do not chase the moving numbers *(amended 2026-09-06: census D; 2026-09-07: R37)* | **v84 · O81** — the night on this Mac needs a predicate, not a habit (E1) | v84 · O81 · §K 15 |

| **W43** | **A nested `claude -p` child CANNOT start its own sandbox under the armed parent, and with `failIfUnavailable: true` it FAILS CLOSED — all Bash refused.** 6 of 6 sandbox-enabled cells, 2 of 2 controls, no exceptions: `EPERM: operation not permitted, listen '/tmp/claude-501/srt-mux-<pid>-1.sock'`. The mechanism is measured rather than inferred — the armed parent's own `bind()`/`listen()` is denied for **unix domain sockets and TCP loopback alike**, errno 1. (LANE C cross-cutting (i), close-C.md) **EXTENDED 2026-09-07 (W50), NOT OVERTURNED: the 6 of 6 is correct for the permission modes it used** — default and `acceptEdits`, which fail closed in a further 7 of 7 cells run by the sandbox panel. **`--permission-mode bypassPermissions` was not among them and behaves differently: 8 of 9 cells read a `denyRead` canary.** Two measurements, two permission modes, **both true** — and a reader who meets either one alone will draw the wrong conclusion about the other, which is why the extension is written here rather than only in W50 | **A FACT AND AN OPEN CONSEQUENCE; no posture is taken here.** Consequence 1: every cell that measures a *child's* sandbox must run with the parent's lifted, so any lane pricing nested dispatch budgets an escalation per cell. Consequence 2: **any design that assumes a dispatched child is itself sandboxed is unmeasured at best.** **The named remedy does not apply:** close-C names `sandbox.enableWeakerNestedSandbox` twice as the untried cure, and the panel's vendor read finds it **LINUX-ONLY** — it bind-mounts a container's existing `/proc` inside unprivileged bubblewrap, and no vendor page speaks to macOS Seatbelt or to a `listen()` denial (review/sandbox-P4-vendor.md part 3). **Nobody has measured it, so this is not *it will fail*; it is *the hope has no page behind it*** — and part 4 of the same review names a likelier macOS-specific candidate, which is the panel's to weigh and not this row's. **§I row 19 is deliberately untouched and the posture decision belongs to the five-lane panel** | applied 2026-09-07 (correction round 2) |

(LANE C cross-cutting (ii), close-C.md) | **v97 · O107 — an OPEN CONSEQUENCE, recorded and not decided.** `SessionStart` is the event #56's shrunken payload rides on, and therefore **the delivery path a ≤ 4,096-byte constitution depends on**. If a dispatched child cannot run it, **the constitution does not reach the child that needs it — and the failure is silent from inside, because the hook FIRES before it fails**, so nothing reports *no constitution*. It also narrows what #56 bought: shrinking the payload made it small enough to arrive, which is necessary and not sufficient. Neither row is amended beyond this note; one cell measuring whether a differently-delivered or unsandboxed child receives the payload is what settles it | applied 2026-09-07 (correction round 2) |

(R1, close-C.md) | **O92 · rule 7 · and this repository's own `.claude/settings.json`.** An `allowWrite`-only policy **grants and never confines** — which is exactly what this repo currently expresses, and it is the measured reason CLAUDE.md's two `**/.worktrees` entries *"do not achieve what they were added for"*. **`isolation: worktree` becomes ENFORCEABLE rather than declared: cwd plus a `denyWrite` list, never an allow list.** Residue, unmeasured: whether a managed tier can pin a `denyWrite` a child cannot widen | applied 2026-09-07 (correction round 2) |

(R32, close-C.md) | **R7's method, and every meter built on it (O95, O115, O116).** A meter reading `usage.*` **scores a compaction as free**, so any cache-share or spend measurement must read `modelUsage` or it is wrong **exactly where compaction happens** — the one place it is most likely to be believed. It also re-reasons **O90**: compaction costs ~292 cache-write tokens, roughly one ordinary turn, and what it really costs is the **loss** of the accumulated cache, 14,522 → 6,163 on the next turn | applied 2026-09-07 (correction round 2) |

Default: **5 cells, 0 reads.** `acceptEdits`: **2 cells, 0 reads.** **No control has read a canary in any round.** Each child's own settings carried `enabled: true`, `failIfUnavailable: true` and the canary in `denyRead`, and **nothing anywhere reported that confinement had been skipped** — Bash runs, the settings file is accepted without complaint, and the cell *looks* like the sandbox working. **The mitigation, measured:** `--restricted` refuses the mode — `exit 1` in **0.1 s, before any model call**, `Error: bypassPermissions not supported in restricted mode` — which is corroboration and mitigation at once, **and it also removes Bash**, so it is not a drop-in for a dispatcher whose child must run commands. (P1 §0, §9.1, §9.2) | **W43 is EXTENDED, not overturned** — close-C's *6 of 6 fail closed* is correct for the modes it used and those modes fail closed here too, so the two results are about **different permission modes and both are true**. **The “undocumented” half is a SEARCH RESULT, not an absence, and must be quoted that way:** `/sandbox…bypassPermissions/` → **0 hits** across the full **610,377-line** string table of 2.1.263, `claude --help` → **0**, and no local vendor doc corpus exists on this machine — but **that lane has no network fetch and says so**, so *we could not find it documented* is the strongest form the evidence carries. That rule is defensible for an interactive session with the founder watching and is **the wrong rule for an unattended fleet at 3 a.m.**; the lane calls it its highest-severity finding. (P2 F5) | **RECORDED AS A PLAN CORRECTION ONLY. `.claude/mcp-policy.json` is NOT edited** — that is the founder's *“change nothing yet”*. The panel's evidence does not carry that sentence and it is not written into the plan here.** What P2 measured is that the two **governed** servers hold **no** credential — which is *why* the policy blocks nothing — and that the credential-holding servers are **absent from the policy entirely**, so there is no false label on them; there is no label at all. **One arguable instance survives and it is one server, not two:** `playwright` is `credentialed: false` in a file whose own `_deny_why` says the browser it drives carries **live session cookies**. **The divergence is written into the row rather than resolved, because resolving it would be inventing the evidence for whichever version reads better** | `.claude/mcp-policy.json` (untouched) · DECISIONS §29 (unedited, divergence noted) · W53 · applied 2026-09-07 (sandbox panel) |

Everything the night is specified to do is T1.** So **the sandbox debate and the night's blockers are DISJOINT SETS.** A designed night does not complete today and **stops twice before the sandbox is consulted**: `ls ~/Library/LaunchAgents/*.plist` returns **eight plists — Google ×3, Perplexity ×3, `com.beamix.clapdetector`, Steam — and none of them is the Watch** (`keel/` does not exist on this branch at all, so `bin/watch`, `bin/run`, `bin/worktree`, `bin/send`, `bin/probe` and `bin/reconcile` are ABSENT in v50's exact sense); and `night_capable` reads **false every night** on `pmset -g custom` → `sleep 1` on both AC and battery, so Gate 9 refuses every `unattended: true` brief before one is minted.
