## N · Research first (R1–R26), the fixer round's questions (R27–R41), the surfaces round (R42), the research close (R43–R44), correction round 2 (R45) and the sandbox panel (R46)

Copied from SYNTHESIS §3. **Each is a bounded question with a source class, and nothing that rests on it can be
decided without it.** Status is **DONE** where the answer is recorded, **OPEN** otherwise.

**R10 is the hinge.** It is the one question whose answer changes the shape of the cross-model architecture
rather than one of its rows: pass, and rung 2 becomes parallel and a second family is a lane; fail, and the
second family is one foreground slot forever, at 1/N availability. **v5, v32, v78, v82 and §I row 15 all read
differently depending on it**, and ~~**R26** — reading the unread Codex June–August window — is the only cheap
route to it~~ **(amended 2026-09-07) — the cheap documentary route was taken and returned nothing, and the
measurement was taken instead and returned everything: R10 is ANSWERED with a condition, `codex exec --json`
runs detached and correct at 0.153.4, but only with the Bash sandbox disabled for that invocation.**

**Nineteen of the forty-four questions moved on 2026-09-07 (the research close), and how they moved is worth
as much as that they moved.** Two lanes and one rehearsal answered twelve documentary questions, five
measurements of this Mac and the terms row. **Read three distinctions or the answers mislead: (1) an
ANSWERED question is not a settled row** — R25 answered and *disqualified* the carrier O52 was pointing at;
R22 answered and left O40 blocked on a different, nameable thing. **(2) NOT FOUND is an answer** — R20
searched and found no roster ablation, which is a fact about the literature and not a failure to look. **(3)
Non-reproduction is not a fix** — R26's answer is *#19945 did not reproduce on this machine at 0.153.4*, and
the June–July changelog window is still unread. **Every lane was single-family, one agent, one pass**, and
four of the findings contradict something this page asserted, so each deserves a second read before it is
relied on further.

| id | The question | Source class | What it decides | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **R1** | Is the Bash sandbox's `filesystem.allowWrite` settable **per `claude -p` invocation**, or project-scoped only? | Vendor sandboxing/settings reference, then one measured cell | Whether the shell can be narrowed to a run's worktree, making `isolation: worktree` **enforced** rather than declared | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-C.md) — YES, and the VERB IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE ONE THIS QUESTION NAMES.** The whole `sandbox.filesystem` block is settable per invocation through `--settings <file>` and it bites. But **`allowWrite` ONLY WIDENS**: it is additive to built-in grants (cwd, temp roots) and cannot take one away — measured, an `allowWrite` naming one directory left a write to a directory outside that list succeeding, and adding the directory changed nothing because both were already covered. **The narrowing verbs are `denyWrite` and `denyRead`**, and both work per invocation: `READ_secret` rc 1 *Operation not permitted*, `WRITE_denied_dir` rc 1, `WRITE_control_dir` rc 0, against a control cell with no deny lists where all three pass. **So `isolation: worktree` CAN be enforced — by cwd plus a `denyWrite` list, never by an allow list** (see **W45**). Residue, unmeasured: whether a managed tier can pin a `denyWrite` a child cannot widen; the binary carries `sandbox.filesystem.allowManagedReadPathsOnly` and one managed-settings cell would close it |
| **R2** | Does the sandbox `credentials` block inject a secret at egress without the child being able to read it, and does `network` support an HTTP-method allowlist and TLS inspection? | Same reference, then one cell | Whether **v68** is one config change or a program we write | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-C.md), three parts: YES · NO · YES.** **Egress credential injection is real** — the child holds a masked placeholder and a proxy substitutes the real credential on the way out — and **TLS inspection exists as `sandbox.network.tlsTerminate` and is its PRECONDITION, not an option beside it**: an unsubstitutable request is *"denied by policy"* in the vendor's own words. **There is NO HTTP-method allowlist — the network policy is domain-scoped.** Keys that exist: `sandbox.credentials.files`, `.awsPairs`, `.sigv4`, `.allowPlaintextInject`, `sandbox.network.tlsTerminate`, `.allowManagedDomainsOnly`, `.deniedDomains`. **Read the source class narrowly: this is DOCUMENTARY, not measured** — verbatim strings from the shipped binary at `/Users/adamks/.local/share/claude/versions/2.1.263`, because `curl` is denied in that session and no doc page was fetched; and the method-allowlist finding is an **absence established by search** (`allowedMethods`, `httpMethods`, `allowMethods`, the verbs), which is weaker than *does not exist*. **Also: masking is partly a MANAGED-settings capability**, so v68 may need an admin act rather than a file in the repo. Residue: one cell with a throwaway masked token and `tlsTerminate` on |
| **R3** | Can a non-interactive background process read a macOS keychain item without an interactive unlock, and under what ACL? | Apple platform documentation, then one detached measurement | Whether §15.4's credential plan works at 3 a.m. at all — **the unattended half rests on it and nothing has tested it**. Also prices **v66**'s keychain-held token | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-C.md) for the read; PARTIAL for the ACL.** A detached, TTY-less child (setsid, `stdin=DEVNULL`) reads an **unlocked** keychain item with **no prompt**. **Locked, it fails INSTANTLY** — rc 152, `errSecInteractionNotAllowed`, well inside a 20 s timeout: **it does not prompt and it does not hang, which is the good failure mode for an unattended lane.** The login keychain here is `no-timeout` and carries **no `lock-on-sleep` flag** — a positive control shows the flag DOES print on a throwaway keychain when set — so **sleep does not lock it**. Corroborated independently by the vendor's own headless supervisor reading the keychain across days in `~/.claude/daemon.log`, and the armed sandbox does **not** block keychain access. **So §15.4's credential plan works at 3 a.m. so long as the machine stays logged in, and the real 3 a.m. risk is NOT the keychain — it is OAuth refresh**, which that same daemon says a headless process cannot complete: *"headless daemon cannot complete OAuth — run `claude auth login`"*. **v66's keychain-held token is priced at zero extra machinery for the read and one founder act for a refresh that expires.** **ACL half PARTIAL**: both items were read by the same `/usr/bin/security` that created them, so the default ACL was never exercised against a different binary. **The launchd context is R33's and is still BLOCKED** |
| **R4** | Can a sandboxed run reach a local model, and in which shape — server inside, server outside, or in-process? | Measurement on this Mac, plus the vendor's network model | Whether the local tier is a service or a program (**O13**). Inbound `bind()` is measured denied; ~~**outbound connect to loopback is unmeasured**~~ **outbound loopback `connect()` is measured denied too (W36)**, so what remains is the in-process shape and the launchd context *(amended 2026-09-06: challenge D P2-8)* | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-C.md) — NO, in no shape a sandboxed run can use.** A local model IS live here (Ollama, `127.0.0.1:11434`, confirmed by `lsof`). A child with `sandbox.enabled: true` cannot reach it by **Bash** (EPERM) **and cannot reach it by WebFetch either** — *"Immediate connect fail for 127.0.0.1: Operation not permitted"* — so **it is not a Bash-only restriction, and a non-Bash tool channel does not route around it**; the same child with the sandbox off gets HTTP 200, 899 bytes. Inbound `bind()`/`listen()` is denied for **unix domain sockets as well as TCP loopback**. **Server-outside is unreachable and server-inside is unstartable: the local tier cannot be a SERVICE**, which confirms O13's shape rather than merely preferring it. **One shape is untested and it is the interesting one — an MCP stdio server**, which the CLI spawns as a child of its own process rather than of the Bash sandbox and which might therefore reach loopback where Bash cannot. Testing it means writing one, which is building, so it was not done; **REVERSED IN PART 2026-09-07 (W47, review/sandbox-P1-measure.md §8.1): ~~NO, in no shape a sandboxed run can use~~ — YES, in two shapes, with one key.** `sandbox.network.allowLocalBinding: true` **alone**, with no localhost entry in `allowedDomains`, returned `CONNECT_OK: HTTP/1.0 200 OK` from that same live Ollama, and bound at three process depths including a `/bin/sh -c` great-grandchild, against a control that denied all five probes and a read canary that stayed denied in the same cell. **Server-outside is reachable and server-inside is startable: the local tier CAN be a service.** **Unreconciled, and left so:** OPEN vendor issue #28018 records the same key failing on the outbound arm for someone else; the lane could not explain the divergence, did not try, and scopes its result to 2.1.263 on this machine. The MCP-stdio shape below is still untested and is no longer the only route — that single cell would settle whether the local tier can be an MCP tool |
| **R5** | How many hours is the Mac actually off, when, and what is the longest single gap? | `pmset -g log` on this machine | Prices **v79** — a cloud lane buying back a small tail is not worth §I row 1 | **DONE 2026-09-06** (DECISIONS §19): span 2026-08-30 21:44 → 2026-09-06 09:39, **156 h · asleep 40.7 h (26%) in 487 episodes · ~~zero episodes of an hour or more~~ · ~~longest single sleep 0.3 h~~**. ~~The tail is **zero** over the week measured.~~ **Caveat, and it is the row's own kind of fact:** the log window is **rolling** — re-read hours later it gave 168 h and 348/77 sleeps (census D, 2026-09-06) — so the span and the episode counts are a reading, ~~the **zero episodes of an hour or more** is what has survived every reading~~, and **R37** is the thirty-day measurement that settles it. Also: only the span the log retains, and DarkWake naps count as wakes. **AMENDED 2026-09-07: R37 falsified the surviving clause, and the reason is ~~that *every reading* meant *every reading of the same seven-day window*~~ THE UNIT, not the window (correction round 1).** **`pmset -g log` retains ~7 days against the ASL store's ~15, which is a real and smaller defect. The defect that matters is that R5 counted SLEEP EPISODES, and Power Nap's 2-second DarkWake every ~16 minutes terminates an episode — so on this machine no episode can reach an hour and *"the tail is zero"* could not have come out any other way.** **Counting CONTIGUOUS SPANS between full `Wake` events over R5's own kind of week (2026-08-31 → 2026-09-07, 166.8 h): 29 spans · 70.0 h · 42% · longest 11.08 h · FOURTEEN ≥ 1 h.** R37's 14.40-day longest of 6 h 41 min is the same error at a longer window, and it surfaced only because that one episode ran with `TCPKeepAlive=inactive` on a flat battery, i.e. **with Power Nap off**. **R5's arithmetic is unchanged and reproduces exactly — 431 episodes, 42.9 h, longest 0.30 h; what does not stand is reading any of it as availability.** **The re-derivation carries a parser trap Lane C hit first and corrected: `pmset -g log` emits `Wake Requests` lines whose first token is also `Wake`, so a regex matching `Wake\s+` closes every span after ~30 seconds and returns 0.3 h instead of 70 h — require `(Sleep\|Wake\|DarkWake)\s{2,}` to separate the column from the prose** |
| **R6** | Which inbound sources document a **polling** read with a cursor, and which need a listening socket? | Vendor API docs, one page per source | Whether the world's door wakes and reads or must be reachable from the internet — **nothing lifts an inbound `bind` under the armed sandbox** | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-W.md): SIX OF SEVEN POLL WITH A CURSOR AND NEED NO INBOUND BIND, so the sandbox's bind denial gates none of them.** Gmail `history.list` from a `startHistoryId` · Calendar `syncToken` · Drive `changes.getStartPageToken` then `changes.list` · Stripe `starting_after` over 30 days of events · GitHub events polled with `ETag`, 304s not counting against the rate limit, 300 events / 30 days retained. **Slack is the interesting one and it also needs no bind**: Socket Mode *"allows your app to use the Events API … without exposing a public HTTP Request URL"*, because the socket is opened **outbound**. **NOTION ALONE NEEDS A PUBLICLY REACHABLE ENDPOINT** — *"It must be a secure (SSL) and publicly available endpoint. Endpoints in localhost are not reachable"* — and it documents **no change feed at all**; its search endpoint carries `start_cursor`/`next_cursor` and sorts by `last_edited_time`, but that is a search, **with no deletion or move events and no completeness guarantee**. Smallest next act, and it is a decision not a fetch: accept a `last_edited_time` poll, or relay Notion's webhook through a cloud Routine API trigger, which needs no local bind |
| **R7** | What is the cache-read share per dispatch shape, and do `--exclude-dynamic-system-prompt-sections` and `--system-prompt-snapshot on` move it? | Ten real moves per shape, read from each run's own token fields, not `/usage` | The premise under **v57**, under §16.3's formula, and under the tenfold reviewer divergence §16.3 records | OPEN |
| **R8** | Does the skill metadata tax scale with the **installed library** or with the agent's **declared namespaces**? | Two trees, identical prompt, input tokens from `--output-format json` | **v77** — and if it scales with namespaces, the budget is unnecessary | OPEN |
| **R9** | Does an unattended `-p` run **auto-compact**, and does it emit anything the log can see? | One long `-p` run with `stream-json --verbose`, plus vendor docs | Whether **v24**, the strongest memory rule, is being broken where it cannot see. If unobservable, the answer is to bound run length | ~~OPEN~~ **ANSWERED by O90 — the run is bounded before it can compact; the remainder is R32** *(amended 2026-09-06: challenge D P2-8)* |
| **R10** | Does `codex exec --json` return output with **no controlling TTY**, on a current version, with a non-trivial prompt? | Five known-answer cases on an installed binary | **The single hinge of the cross-model architecture.** Pass and rung 2 becomes parallel; fail and the second family is one foreground slot forever. Note **W19**: the version floor must be restated | **ANSWERED WITH A CONDITION, 2026-09-07 (review/codex-rehearsal.md) — and the condition is ours, not Codex's.** **Yes, it returns output with no controlling TTY**: 20/20 exit 0 with non-empty correct-shaped stdout, 10 fully detached, `--json` giving four well-formed events every run (`thread.started` → `turn.started` → `item.completed` → `turn.completed`), `websocket connected (HTTP 101)`, a second family answering real review questions. **Reachable ONLY with the Bash sandbox disabled for that invocation.** R10's honest form is now ***"reachable, conditional on a sandbox exemption that does not exist yet"*** — `denyRead` on `~/.codex` plus provider TLS at the egress proxy, **two blockers, either sufficient, not separated by this evidence** because disabling the sandbox removed both together. **That exemption is a founder policy decision, not a Codex property and not an agent's call: §I row 19.** **What the hinge still does not turn on:** all 20 runs were **serial**, so **concurrency is unmeasured → R44**, and that is what now stands between Codex and a night lane |
| **R11** | Does a second model family reduce escaped defects on **our** move classes, and by how much? | Twenty-five paired checks from our own event log | Whether rung 2 outranks rung 4 on any given class, and contrarian assumption 2. **Blocked on O7's four provenance fields — cheap now, unreconstructable later** | OPEN |
| **R12** | What fraction of a venture's real done-tests reach rung 1 **without inventing an anchor**? | The harness venture's first thirty intents | **Contrarian assumption 1 (§I row 16), and with it the shape of the whole architecture.** The cheapest measurement named anywhere in the round | OPEN |
| **R13** | Does `LICENSE-CONTENT` permit **derivative** skill bodies, not merely redistribution? | The licence file, read raw | Narrower than §I row 4 and decisive: under v18 we would **rewrite** imported bodies, which is a derivative work MIT-on-the-code says nothing about | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-W.md), and NARROWER THAN THE QUESTION ASSUMED — the fetch §I row 4 was waiting for is done.** `LICENSE-CONTENT`, read raw: *"Original documentation and other non-code written content are licensed under the Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International license unless a more specific upstream notice says otherwise"*, with *"Third-party attributions, upstream licenses, and exceptions … tracked in `docs/sources/sources.md`"*. CC BY 4.0 §2(a)(1) grants the right to *"produce, reproduce, and Share Adapted Material"* and §3(a) requires you *"indicate if You modified the Licensed Material"*. **So rewriting the repository's OWN bodies is permitted with attribution plus a modification notice — and that is the whole grant.** **BULK VENDORING AND REWRITING THE IMPORTED 2,111 IS NOT CLEARED BY THIS FILE**: `sources.md` lists roughly fifty upstreams under MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-3-Clause, **CC-BY-SA** (ShareAlike binds any derivative), **"Proprietary (official vendor skills)"** (grants nothing) and **"Not declared upstream"** (an absence of grant, not a permission); one GPLv3 component was excluded upstream on purpose. **Residue, and it is the act before any import: tally `sources.md` by its licence column** — which skills sit under CC-BY-SA, Proprietary or undeclared, and how many of each. Confidence: high on the licence text, medium on the source count, read rendered rather than counted |
| **R14** | Do any of the three CLIs emit a **skill-activation event** we can read? | The three vendors' hook and telemetry docs | Whether **O41** is instrumentation or a field lookup | **PARTIAL 2026-09-07 (close-W.md) — no dedicated event in ANY of the three, and a likely carrier that is inference rather than quotation, so O41 and O114 STAY `DEPENDS-ON-R14`.** Claude Code documents 33 hook events, none skill-specific; its matcher table gives *"`PreToolUse` … \| tool name"*, and `Skill` is a real tool (*"Permission syntax: `Skill(name)` for exact match"*), **so PreToolUse is very likely the carrier — but the docs never name `Skill` as a matcher, and that step is the inference.** Codex documents 12 hook events, none skill-specific; Gemini CLI's telemetry defines `tool_call`, `slash_command`, `extension_enable`/`disable` and no skill event. **The residue is one measured cell, and it is cheap: register a PreToolUse hook with matcher `Skill`, invoke a skill, see whether it fires.** High confidence on the absence, medium on the carrier |
| **R15** | Is there any published measurement of **selection accuracy as installed-skill count rises**? | Spec authors, vendor engineering posts, the harness showcase | The real risk of importing thousands. If nobody has measured it, **v77**'s budget is what bounds the exposure | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-W.md) — YES, TWICE, AND THIS REPO IS ALREADY PAST THE MEASURED THRESHOLD.** *"performance degrades as libraries grow—by up to 21% when scaling from a small set of helpful skills to a 202-skill library"* — libraries 52 / 102 / 202, pass-rate drop 0.08 (95% CI 0.02–0.15) / 0.14 (0.09–0.19) / 0.21 (0.15–0.27), **significant from 102** (arXiv:2605.24050v2). And *"as pools grow from 5 to 100, actual-use precision falls from 29.6% to 3.3%"*, with top-1 embedding precision 88.3% → 76.9% and explicit selection 70.0% → 63.7% (arXiv:2608.14036v1). **This repo carries 134 (W40).** The second paper names the mechanism: **semantically similar skills competing for selection**, which is what a bulk import of overlapping upstreams produces. **THE LIMIT, IN THE SAME BREATH: neither paper tests beyond 202, so the 2,111 import is UNMEASURED, not measured-bad** — nobody has measured a library that size, and v77's budget is now one of two things bounding the exposure rather than the only one |
| **R16** | Does any shipped agent system carry a **scheduling object between the goal and the run** — two objects or three? | Symphony, Linear's agent session model, Copilot's task object; source read | **O1**'s shape; cognition.md's ticket table is explicitly incomplete here | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-W.md): TWO OF THREE SHIP TWO OBJECTS, ONE SHIPS THREE — so O1's two-object shape is the majority and is defensible.** OpenAI Symphony makes the issue itself the schedulable thing (*"Normalized schedulable work item used by orchestration…"*) with a Run Attempt beside it, and **scheduling is a loop rather than an object** — it *"schedules an immediate tick, and then repeats every `polling.interval_ms`"*. Linear ships issue → agent session → agent activity, event-driven with no time-based object. **GitHub Copilot is the exception and ships a third**: a task created and listed independently of any issue, carrying `queued`, `in_progress`, `completed`, `failed`, `idle`, `waiting_for_user`, `timed_out`, `cancelled`. **The one system that ships three does so to carry a queue across a NETWORK BOUNDARY between caller and runner, which this design does not have** — which is why the majority shape is also the right one here rather than merely the popular one. High on Symphony and Linear; medium on Copilot, read off the endpoint shape and the state list |
| **R17** | What does `/goal`'s evaluator accept as a condition, and does an **exit-code** condition behave differently from a prose one? | Vendor doc plus one measured pair of runs | **O21**, which is otherwise a design argument. Now also bounded by **W14**'s three-check-in cap | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-C.md), and it is the sharpest single finding of that lane.** `/goal` takes **one free-text string and nothing else** — no condition type, no syntax, **no exit-code form** — and **the evaluator is a MODEL READING THE TRANSCRIPT, not a command runner. It never executes the condition**, so an exit-code condition and a prose condition are handled identically; the exit-code arm was judged **met** on the strength of what the transcript narrated, with `test -f` never run. Record shape: `{"type":"goal_status","condition":<verbatim string>,"met":bool,"reason":str,"iterations":int,"durationMs":int,"tokens":int}`, plus `"sentinel":true` on the first record. **~1,500 tokens and ~21 s per evaluation**, and **a goal does not survive `--resume`**. **This settles O21 and settles it AGAINST `/goal` as a gate**, and it falsified **v12**, which had written the exit-code reading into the plan as the cure for judging by prose. Residue: whether the evaluator behaves differently in an interactive session where check-ins actually fire |
| **R18** | Is there measured evidence for a **re-attempt ceiling across sessions**? | Vendor docs and any measured paper on repeated-attempt yield | The N in the attempt counter (**O73**), which is otherwise taste | **PARTIAL 2026-09-07 (close-W.md) — measured evidence exists for retry ceilings WITHIN a context, not ACROSS sessions, and it cuts in this design's favour.** *"When an LLM agent fails a multi-step tool-augmented task and retries, the failed attempt typically remains in its context window—contaminating the next attempt"*; on SWE-bench Verified **pass@1 = 0.761, pass@3 = 0.812**, while *"the IID model overestimates pass@3 by 17.4 percentage points (98.6% vs. 81.2%)"*; and *"Context-clearing before retry strictly reduces required attempts"* at an **improvement ratio ≈1.21 — *"same budget resolves 21% more tasks"*** (arXiv:2605.08563v1). **N = 3 has measured support; N above 3 has none that was found.** **And because a new session starts clean, an across-session re-attempt IS the arm the paper shows is better — so the ceiling argument supports O73's shape rather than opposing it.** Bound: the paper does not test across-session yield, so transferring the numbers is medium confidence and no across-session measurement was found |
| **R19** | Does an **onboarding pack** change an agent's anchored pass rate? | Our own with-against-baseline runner, run locally | **v71**, and whether wave two should be gated on evidence at all | OPEN |
| **R20** | Has anyone published a **roster-size ablation between six and one hundred and fifty**? | Google ADK, LangGraph templates, Sakana, Cognition's and Factory's rosters, TheAgentCompany's leaderboard | The only fact that could responsibly move **v31** in either direction. **W30** closed the headline; the leaderboard remains unobtained | **ANSWERED AS NOT FOUND 2026-09-07 (close-W.md) — and NOT FOUND is the answer, not a failure to look.** Searched TheAgentCompany (paper, README, site), Google ADK, LangGraph templates, Sakana, Cognition and Factory roster material, AgentArch, MegaAgent, MultiAgentBench and the arXiv multi-agent scaling literature: **no published ablation varies roster size across the 6–150 range.** The closest measured curve stops at **eight**: *"we vary the number of agents n from 1 to 8 in increments of 1"*, concluding *"MAS performance does not scale monotonically with agent count but follows a pattern of diminishing returns"* and *"task type critically modulates the optimal agent count"* — Philosophy peaking at 98.04% (n=4,6), Formal Logic collapsing from 58.82% at one agent to 25.49% (arXiv:2606.00655v1). **So v31 cannot responsibly be moved by external evidence in EITHER direction, which is what v31 already says about itself.** **The leaderboard is unobtained for a second recorded time** — the site is a client-rendered SPA that returns only a page title to a text fetch, and the GitHub README links it rather than containing it; it needs a JavaScript-rendering fetch, which is designer-shaped with Playwright and not `WebFetch` |
| **R21** | Does any **credit-spending server expose a spend or balance read**? | Vendor API docs, one fetch each | **O32**: with a balance read a program can hold an absolute ceiling and Higgsfield stays in WRITES; without one it belongs in REACHES-THE-WORLD | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-W.md), SPLIT — and the split runs OPPOSITE to §F's assignment.** **RunPod exposes the read**: `myself` publishes `clientBalance`, `currentSpendPerHr`, `minBalance`, `underBalance`, `clientLifetimeSpend`, **`spendLimit`**, `creditAlertThreshold`, `spendDetails` and `dailyCharges` (graphql-spec.runpod.io). **Higgsfield documents none** — its FAQ points only at a dashboard: *"You can monitor your API usage, credit consumption, and request history through the Higgsfield Cloud dashboard."* **Applied to the rule as written: Higgsfield moves to REACHES-THE-WORLD and RunPod's refusal loses its stated reason (§F, O32, §B.2 `writer`).** Residue, cheap and unspent: a secondary source claims the Higgsfield **MCP server** exposes balance and per-model pricing **as tools**; a `tools/list` read would settle it and **spends nothing**. The lane declined it only because it could not judge whether a tool listing crossed its no-authentication brief |
| **R22** | What does **page 3 cost to render at a year of rows**, and where is the knee? | Synthesised event log at this Mac's own emission rate, measured against the real server | Whether **O40** is a day-one shape or a later migration touching the one store the design says is never edited | **ANSWERED (the knee) · PARTIAL (the real server), 2026-09-07 (close-M.md) — and the answer is that the TIMING does not decide O40.** **0.98 µs/row under Bun 1.3.10, 1.10 under Node 24.11.1; the 1-second knee is ~1.06 M rows** (`bun … ms = 0.000979 × rows − 42.5`; node `0.001098 × rows − 33.6`, crossing at 941,309). **In time that is 7.7 years at this Mac's `events.jsonl` rate (377 rows/day) and 8 months at its model-call rate (4,287/day).** One year renders in **110 ms** at the first and **1,495 ms** at the second; **O40's own shape — one month at the model-call rate, 130,396 rows — renders in 102 ms**, 14.6× cheaper than the year. Scaling is mildly **super**linear (0.80 → 0.97 µs/row) as the per-run map grows to 428,572 entries at 3 M rows. **THE RESIDUE THAT MATTERS IS NOT A MEASUREMENT: write down which rate page 3's log emits at.** Nothing in §D, §16.1a or O40 says whether a row is a run, a step or a model call, **and the answer moves the knee by 11×** — that undecided rate, not the timing, is what O40 turns on. **Two bounds:** every figure is a **warm-cache floor** (a cold 542 MiB read adds I/O this cell did not pay), and it was **not measured against the real server** — `mission-control/node_modules` does not exist and installing was refused, so HTTP, serialisation and paint sit on top of these numbers. **The control that lets the timing be read as timing: both runtimes returned byte-identical aggregates at every size** (`spend_usd 496724.01`, `cache_hit 0.9439`, `distinct_runs 428572` at 3 M rows) |
| **R23** | Is there a **concurrency ceiling on `claude -p` children**, and does the N+1th fail loudly, queue, or degrade silently? | Vendor docs first, then one measurement | **O71** — the board refuses a drag that would breach a bound, and no bound is a session count | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (research/close-C.md), and the answer to *"fail loudly, queue, or degrade silently"* is DEGRADE SILENTLY.** No ceiling exists up to 10 — 10 of 10 succeeded, none failed, none queued — and at **N=20 all twenty still succeeded while the machine swapped 8,280 pages**, so the N+1th never announces itself. **Nothing in the CLI enforces a local session count:** `--capacity`, the only *"Max concurrent sessions"* flag, belongs to the **self-hosted cloud runner** (*"--capacity has no effect on a session-bound runner"*), and the vendor's only other concurrency concept is the **Agent tool's** `concurrency_limit` for subagents, not OS-level children. **So the bound has to be the board's own (O71), and the failure mode to design for is not a loud refusal but a seven-day rate-limit window at 0.68 with `"overageStatus": "rejected"`** |
| **R24** | Does **pixel-agents** render ten venture areas and fifty live agents, and what is its ~~`AgentEvent`~~ **wire** schema and ingest rate? | The project's own source and tracker, then one local run | **v62**'s UNVERIFIED, and the writer §14.4 says is ours to build | **PARTIAL 2026-09-07 (close-W.md): schema and ingest ANSWERED from source; the render claim needs the local run, which is an install and was refused.** **THERE IS NO `AgentEvent` TYPE IN THE SOURCE** — the README's *"The Claude provider normalizes both sources into a shared `AgentEvent` model"* names a model the code does not define, and neither `core/src/messages.ts` nor `core/src/schemas.ts` declares it. **The real wire model is a message union** (`AgentCreated`, `AgentClosed`, `AgentStatus`, `AgentToolStart`/`Done`, `SubagentToolStart`/`Done`, `AgentContextUsage`, `AreaMappingsLoaded`, `HooksStatus`, … 31 members). **Areas are first-class** (`AreaMappingsLoaded`, `SaveAreaMappings`, `SetShowAreas`). **Ingest is poll-based**: `JSONL_POLL_INTERVAL_MS = 1000`, `FILE_WATCHER_POLL_INTERVAL_MS = 500`, `PROJECT_SCAN_INTERVAL_MS = 1000`, `HOOK_EVENT_BUFFER_MS = 5000`, `MAX_HOOK_BODY_SIZE = 65536`. **NO constant anywhere caps agents, seats, areas or grid size** — the only numeric limits in `core` are display truncations. **So ten areas and fifty agents are FORBIDDEN BY NOTHING AND DEMONSTRATED BY NOTHING: unmeasured, and the distinction is the point.** The act that closes it: whoever may install runs it, seeds ten areas and fifty agents, and counts |
| **R25** | What are **`/schedule`**'s semantics — interval floor, storage, headless status? | The vendor doc page, which returned **HTTP 404** on 2026-09-06 — **and again on 2026-09-07: it is a DEAD PATH, not a transient one, and the answer lives at /docs/en/routines and /docs/en/scheduled-tasks** | Whether **v55**'s Watch-dispatched standing intents should route through it or ignore it | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-W.md): IGNORE IT — `/schedule` is DISQUALIFIED for v55, and the doc that 404'd is not where the answer lives.** `/schedule` is the CLI entry to **cloud Routines**: *"The minimum interval is one hour; expressions that run more frequently are rejected"* · account-stored, *"Create and manage them at claude.ai/code/routines"* · *"Routines execute on Anthropic-managed cloud infrastructure … so they keep working when your laptop is closed"* · **local files: "No (fresh clone)"** · *"`/schedule` requires a claude.ai subscription login"* and *"API accounts aren't supported"* · runs are **staggered**, *"a few minutes after the scheduled time"*, with a consistent offset. **It is untouched by this Mac sleeping because it never runs on this Mac, so the catch-up question does not arise for it — and "No (fresh clone)" is what disqualifies it, because a standing intent needs THIS machine's files.** **The replacement is the Desktop scheduled task**: 1-minute floor, no open session needed, `~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/<task-name>/SKILL.md`. **It also closes O52's fear as CONFIRMED rather than feared** — *"If your computer sleeps through a scheduled time, the run is skipped"*, and on wake *"Desktop starts exactly one catch-up run for the most recently missed time and discards anything older."* Relevant to R41 in passing: there is a **"Keep computer awake"** setting under Desktop app → General, and *"Closing the laptop lid still puts it to sleep"* |
| **R26** | Was **#19945** fixed between Codex 0.125 and 0.153? | The unread June–August changelog window; three fetch failures recorded | It would settle **R10** without a local install, and **it is the only cheap route to it** | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 — BY MEASUREMENT, NOT BY A CHANGELOG, and the two must never be written as one.** **The finding is *non-reproduction at 0.153.4 on this machine*: 10 of 10 detached invocations (`setsid`, no controlling terminal, stdin `/dev/null`) returned exit 0 with a correct non-empty answer, and 20 of 20 across all cells** (review/codex-rehearsal.md). **It is NOT *"fixed in 0.1xx"*, and the June–July window is STILL UNREAD** — the documentary route (close-W.md) found the three prior failures were a **308** to learn.chatgpt.com nobody followed, that the readable Codex CLI entries run **0.149.0 → 0.153.4**, and that **there are no June or July 2026 Codex CLI entries on that page at all**, so 0.125–0.148 is empty on the source rather than unfetched. **The issue is open today**, created 2026-04-28, never closed, confirmed via the issue page and the search API; two TTY-adjacent changelog entries name a different issue (#40460, #41436) and none of nine merged detached-related PRs addresses it. **Non-reproduction is consistent with a fix AND with the bug being conditional on something this machine does not have** — the measurement replaces the need to read the window rather than reading it, and **that bound travels with this finding everywhere it appears** |

**R27–R40 (fixer round)** are merged from the three fixer files; **R41 was added 2026-09-06 by challenge D P1-3**, and it is the one question that decides whether the night has a founder act in front of it. Dropped by overrule: A's *ssh to a box* (E1) and
C's *vanilla-week numbers* (E14). **R43 and R44 were added 2026-09-07 by the research close, and each stands
where an answer emptied something:** R43 where R40 removed v90's Gemini route, R44 where the Codex rehearsal
discharged v32's detachment condition and left concurrency in its place. **Neither reopens a founder row** —
R43 asks about a CLI on an account and not a key, and R44 asks about a lane's shape and not its admission.
**R45 was minted 2026-09-07 in correction round 2 and it is different in kind from both: it DECIDES an open
founder row.** *Does a `-p` run make progress during a DarkWake* had been living inside R37's re-specified
form and inside v79's caveat, named in two places as the thing that resolves the 43–70 h range to a point.
**A question that decides a founder row should be findable by its own id**, so it has one.

| id | The question | Source class | What it decides | Status |

| **R27** | **R12, split three ways:** (a) the harness's first thirty; (b) thirty random Floor episodes, domain-labelled by one Sonnet `-p` pass, rated by the founder in one sitting — *could a program have judged this?* — plus thirty paper done-tests across §23–§30; (c) the second venture's first thirty. *Inventing an anchor* = one that tests a property the done-test did not state, reads a record the company writes, or would pass a rejected artifact | the corpus (tainted episodes excluded), one sitting under O95 | **§I row 16, by domain** — (b) and (c) together, never (a) alone (O102) | OPEN — (b) zero build |
| **R28** | Does `--restricted` ignore `--settings <file>` as it ignores the settings files? | one measured cell | **O87** | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-C.md) — NO: `--settings <file>` STILL APPLIES under `--restricted`.** The vendor says so (*"managed settings and --settings still apply"*) and five cells confirm it, **with a positive control that proves the cell can see the difference**: the same `{"model": "claude-haiku-4-5"}` payload delivered through a **project** settings file IS ignored under `--restricted` — the run falls back to `claude-opus-5[1m]` — while the same payload through `--settings` is honoured. The observable is `modelUsage` in `--output-format json` with no `--model` flag passed, so nothing else could move it. **So `--restricted` and `--settings` COMPOSE: a launcher can drop the user, project and local tiers and still deliver exactly the policy it intends, in one file — `--restricted` does not have to be traded against configurability.** Untested and adjacent: whether a managed settings file overrides `--settings` as it overrides everything else |
| **R29** | RSS per `-p` child and the page-in point on this Mac | ~~`memory_pressure` from launchd under N children~~ **`vm_stat`'s `Swapouts` counter, differenced across each cell — and the instrument is half the finding (see status)** | **O71**'s `sessions_ceiling`; ~~3 until measured (THINKER: A14)~~ **17, measured** | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (research/close-C.md addendum): THE KNEE IS BETWEEN 17 AND 20 CONCURRENT `-p` CHILDREN, and the machine DEGRADES SILENTLY rather than refusing.** **N=17 pages not at all; N=20 writes 8,280 pages (136 MB) to swap — and all 20 children still SUCCEEDED**, which also answers R23's open half. Per-child RSS *falls* as N rises, 247 MB at N=1 to a 229 MB median at N=20, because the children are being trimmed under pressure. **THE INSTRUMENT IS THE OTHER HALF OF THE ANSWER AND MUST TRAVEL WITH THE NUMBER: `Swapouts` is the only counter here that fires — 0 at every N through 17, then 8,280. Free-memory percentage MOVES THE WRONG WAY ACROSS THE KNEE (45% at N=20 against 44% at N=14) and would have missed it entirely; `Pages free` is no better, its minimum at N=4 (268 MB) being higher than at N=1 (86 MB).** **A ceiling with no instrument that detects its crossing is exactly what this plan refuses**, so O71 records both. Recovery was complete within 25 s. **Confidence: high for the bracket** (single clean crossing) **and medium for the exact number** — one crossing, one machine state, not repeated, **with eight resident `claude` sessions already holding 2.16 GB. *The knee is 20* and *the knee is 20 given 2.16 GB resident* are different numbers, and the second is the one the board needs** |
| **R30** | Does `--no-session-persistence` suppress the `~/.claude/projects` JSONL for a `-p` child? | a canary in a fixture inbound row, `grep -r` | **O88 · v93**'s *settled by* | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-C.md) — YES for the conversation JSONL, NO for the project directory.** With the flag no session file is written and the canary appears in no child transcript; without it `<session-id>.jsonl` is written under the cwd-slug directory and holds the canary. **A `memory/` subdirectory is created either way**, so the residual leak is that a session RAN in that cwd, never what it said. **Two failure modes of the naive version are identified and avoided, and both are worth carrying**: a `grep -r` for the canary also hits the measuring lane's OWN transcript, which is contamination, so the finding rests on the by-session-id lookup; and an earlier sandboxed attempt was **void, not negative** — the armed parent denies writes to `~/.claude/projects`, so neither arm could persist and the control was not a control. Residue: whether `memory/` ever receives content under the flag was not tested; it was empty in both arms |
| **R31** | The daemon's lease semantics, idle exit, and what `claude agents --json` lists after it exits | `daemon.log`, one `--bg` child | **O91** — `bin/supervise` REFUSED until answered | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-C.md), end to end including teardown.** The daemon is started on demand by `claude --bg` (`origin=transient`), holds **a lease per worker in `~/.claude/daemon/roster.json`'s `workers` map** keyed by short id — `pid`, `procStart`, `sessionId`, `cliVersion`, `attempt`, `rendezvousSock`, `ptySock`, `cwd` and the full `dispatch` record — keeps **one warm spare** ahead of demand, and **exits 5 s after the last client**, logging `cause=idle_exit, leases=0, live_workers=0`. After `rm` the job is listed **nowhere**: `listed_after_rm=[]`, `roster_workers=[]` at every poll. Two further surfaces: `claude agents --json` works with **no TTY and does not start the daemon**, and `--all` returns jobs from months back out of `~/.claude/jobs/`, so the record outlives the daemon. **The load-bearing constraint: `--bg` REFUSES `-p`** — *"--bg and --print conflict: --print never starts the interactive session that `claude agents` attaches to"* — so a supervised background session is an **interactive** session, with everything that implies for reading its output (`claude logs <id>` returns raw terminal output, ANSI included). Residue: lease **expiry** was never observed — the worker was killed, not left to time out |
| **R32** | Does `PreCompact` fire under `-p`, and does a compaction cost a 2x cache write? | one long `-p` run, `stream-json --verbose` | **O90**; the rest of R9 | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-C.md), both halves, and the second half inverts the reason rather than the rule.** **`PreCompact` FIRES under `-p`** — and it fires *before* the decision to compact, so it fires even when compaction is then refused (*"Not enough messages to compact."* with `PreCompact.fired: YES`). Every other hook tested under `-p` fired too (`UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`, `SessionEnd`); `SessionStart` fired and then FAILED, which is **W44**. **A compaction does NOT cost a 2x cache write: it cost 292 cache-creation tokens against a 3,401–4,958 steady state — roughly one ordinary turn. It costs a CACHE LOSS**, and the loss lands on the *next* turn: accumulated cache read collapses 14,522 → 6,163, the base, reproduced across two runs. **And the cost is INVISIBLE in `usage`, which reports all zeros for the compacting turn — only `modelUsage` carries it** (**W46**), so a meter reading `usage.*` scores a compaction as free. Residue: whether **auto**-compaction (`--autocompact <tokens>`) reports the same way, and what `PreCompact`'s payload distinguishes between the two triggers |
| **R33** | Can a LaunchAgent on THIS Mac read a keychain item after sleep, and after reboot with the screen locked? | one detached read across `pmset sleepnow`, one after reboot | **R3's unattended half**, critical under E1; `--bare` skips keychain reads | **BLOCKED 2026-09-07 (close-C.md) — and the blocker MOVED: it is now the permission system, not scope.** The LaunchAgent was not created: the plist was refused **twice** by the Claude Code auto mode classifier — *"Permission for this action was denied by the Claude Code auto mode classifier. Reason: Blocked by classifier."* — once as XML and once via `plistlib`, **to a scratchpad path, so it is the ARTIFACT being refused and not the location**. The lane stopped after the second refusal rather than looking for a third encoding, and **its reasoning is recorded verbatim because it is correct behaviour this plan should teach, not merely a blocked question: *"The founder's authorisation was relayed to me by a teammate, and a teammate's message is not what grants capability here; the permission system is, and it said no."*** Nothing was registered and `~/Library/LaunchAgents` is untouched. **What it settled anyway:** the login keychain carries no `lock-on-sleep` flag (positive control), so **sleep does not lock it**, and a detached TTY-less sampler took 16 clean readings over 4m41s — **but no sleep episode fell in that window, so it is a baseline and not the sleep measurement**. **OWNER: the founder, two acts of different sizes.** (1) The launchd context — either a Bash permission rule allowing `*.plist` writes, or the founder runs `plutil -lint`, `launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u)` and `launchctl bootout` themselves; the probe is already written. (2) **The reboot-with-screen-locked case, which no permission fix reaches** — it needs a real reboot, and R3's matrix **predicts an immediate `errSecInteractionNotAllowed` there**, because the login keychain has never been unlocked at that point. That prediction is the hypothesis the experiment should try to falsify, and it decides whether a night can start unattended after a power cut |
| **R34** | The founder's measured decision throughput: whiches and `AskUserQuestion` rounds answered per day | `~/.claude/projects/` snapshot, counted | **O96**'s seed | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-M.md), and the AMBIGUITY IT EXPOSED IS THE FINDING, not the number.** **3.09 answered `AskUserQuestion` rounds and 8.68 questions per active day** (median 2.5 / 6.5, p90 6 / 17, max 8 / 24) — 68 answered rounds, 191 answered questions, 22 active days in a 41-day span; time to answer median 217 s, p90 921 s. **Measured against O96's own predicate, the peak in any rolling five-hour window is 7 rounds and 16 questions.** **So O96's seed of six is about right if a *which* is one ROUND — it would have refused once in 41 days — and 2.7× too small if a *which* is one QUESTION. O96 does not say which unit it means.** **A second fact worth as much: the founder REJECTS 81% of rounds put to them, 291 of 359** (*"The user doesn't want to proceed with this tool use"*), so **throughput is not the binding constraint — relevance is**, and a budget on how many whiches *open* is aimed at the wrong end. Also: 39 `ExitPlanMode` uses, 35 approved, 1.75 approved plans per active day. **Method note that returned a confident false zero on the first run: an `AskUserQuestion` result record does not contain the string `AskUserQuestion`**, so a one-pass grep finds every question and no answer. Uncounted: which-shaped exchanges held in prose, which leave no tool record — R27(b)'s sitting is where those get labelled. Full census of the corpus, not a sample |
| **R35** | Cumulative shadow USD of unattended work on this seat against the seat price, today | ~~`~/.agentvibe/events.jsonl`, priced by §9.6~~ **the named source, which turned out not to carry it** | **O116**'s first reading; the size of the bet on §I row 1 | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-M.md) — AS A REFUTATION OF ITS OWN NAMED SOURCE.** **`~/.agentvibe/events.jsonl` carries no price inputs at all** — a full census of all 3,843 rows returns two keys matching `token\|cost\|usd\|model\|price`, and **`window_output` is the constant 5000 across all 1,422 records that carry it: a fixture value, not a gauge.** So §9.6 cannot price it, today or ever in this schema. **READ STRICTLY, O116's first line is `$0.00 ÷ $100 = 0.0×`, because ZERO unattended runs have ever happened — and that is what should be printed.** **The number O116 will eventually print does exist elsewhere, and it is a SHADOW PRICE in v23's exact sense — a locally computed shadow of a bill nobody sends, NEVER spend:** 180,053 priced model calls in `~/.claude/projects` over **42 calendar days** at §9.6 list = **$27,069.46**, ≈$19,619/month against a $100 seat. **Both bounds:** the seat price is the *"From $100 per month"* the plan itself marks UNVERIFIED, so the multiple is an **upper** bound; **6.1% of output tokens sit on retired model ids §9.6 has no row for**, so the dollar figure is a **floor**. **The two nearest proxies are proxies, and both are labelled:** sidechain 59%, night 00:00–06:59 17% — **a subagent also runs while the founder watches, and this founder demonstrably works past midnight.** **Free residue, and it is the *cheap now, unreconstructable later* case: `unattended` has no marker in either store — write `unattended: true` into the run record at dispatch**, in O7's provenance fields |

| **R37** | The Mac's off-hours and longest gap over thirty days including a weekend away, as a floor — ~~counted as sleep episodes~~ **counted as CONTIGUOUS SPANS BETWEEN FULL `Wake` EVENTS, reporting the Sleep/DarkWake split inside every span (RE-SPECIFIED 2026-09-07, correction round 1).** **The question is re-specified in its own text because the old form is not merely imprecise, it is unanswerable: Power Nap's 2-second DarkWake every ~16 minutes terminates a sleep episode, so thirty days counted as episodes would return *"zero episodes of an hour or more"* again — correctly, and uselessly.** A run of the old form is not evidence and must not be recorded as any | **O82** over ~~`pmset -g log`~~ **`/var/log/powermanagement/*.asl` — `pmset -g log` is the source that produced three wrong readings** | **§I row 15** on a month; **§I 18 / §J 73**'s `wins_if:` | **OPEN — RE-SPECIFIED 2026-09-07 (correction round 1), NOT ANSWERED.** **Read the two readings below as provenance for why the question changed, never as its answer.** **The span re-count (research/close-C.md), over 2026-08-31 10:35:17 → 2026-09-07 09:21:22, 166.8 h: 29 spans · 70.0 h · 42% of the week · longest 11.08 h · FOURTEEN spans ≥ 1 h**, against **431 episodes · 42.9 h · longest 0.30 h · zero ≥ 1 h** on the identical log and window. **Same data, two units, and only one of them can answer this question.** The longest span is verified event by event — 2026-09-03 00:09:42 → 11:14:36, **one full `Wake` and 17 DarkWakes**. **The split that decides what the number means: 42.9 h true Sleep (61%) and 27.1 h DarkWake (39%), swinging night to night (85% DarkWake on 09-03, 94% true sleep on 09-07) — and WHETHER A `claude -p` RUN MAKES PROGRESS DURING A DARKWAKE IS UNMEASURED, so the weekly tail is a RANGE, 43–70 h.** Settling it is a bounded process timestamping a line every 60 s across one night, read back against `pmset -g log` — **a founder act, and a smaller one than the LaunchAgent R33 needs.** **Parser trap, recorded because the lane hit it first: `pmset -g log` emits `Wake Requests` lines whose first token is also `Wake`; a regex matching `Wake\s+` closes every span after ~30 s and returns 0.3 h instead of 70 h. Require `(Sleep\|Wake\|DarkWake)\s{2,}`.** **The earlier reading, now superseded and kept for the progression: PARTIAL (close-M.md) — 14.40 DAYS OF THE 30 ASKED, NO WEEKEND AWAY IN THE WINDOW.** Over **345.6 h / 14.40 days** (2026-08-23 21:06Z → 2026-09-07 06:40Z) the machine slept **68.23 h, 19.7%, across 633 episodes**, and **the longest single gap is 6 h 41 min** — 2026-08-28 00:48:20Z → 07:28:56Z, cause `'Low Power Sleep'`, waking on AC at **1% charge. The battery ran flat.** **The distribution is bimodal and that is the useful shape: one episode of 400.7 minutes, second-longest 18.0 minutes**, with 482 Maintenance Sleeps, 115 back-to-sleep and 34 clamshell over the window. **"Zero episodes of an hour or more" is TRUE of the seven-day `pmset` window and FALSE of this machine, and W33, R5 and census D all read only that window** — `pmset -g log` retains ~7 days, `/var/log/powermanagement` retains ~15, and `pmset` does not show the difference. **THE NIGHT ON THIS MAC IS BOUNDED BY THE BATTERY, NOT BY MAINTENANCE SLEEP**, which is what moves O81's predicate to AC-and-charge-first. **Confidence: high on the 14.40-day window** (full parse, sleep/wake pairing cross-checked against log-coverage silence — the 400.6-minute silence and the 400.7-minute episode are the same event seen two ways; timestamps normalised to UTC through each line's own offset so a timezone shift cannot manufacture a gap). **Low on extrapolating to thirty days: one flat battery in fourteen is n=1.** **R37 CANNOT BE CLOSED BY READING — the store rotates at ~15 days, the other sixteen days are already gone, and it needs collection to start (O82). The weekend away is not schedulable and must be waited for.** **And that earlier reading is itself an UNDERESTIMATE, produced by the same episode-counting error at a different window length: its 6 h 41 min is the one episode Power Nap did not break, because `TCPKeepAlive=inactive` on a flat battery means Power Nap was off** |

| **R40** | Do Google's terms permit automated access through the Gemini CLI on a personal account, for checking? | Google's terms, read raw (§G.5) | **v90** — under E7 there is no key alternative | **ANSWERED 2026-09-07 (close-W.md), AND THE ANSWER MOOTS THE TERMS QUESTION: the route does not exist.** **Google withdrew personal-account access to the Gemini CLI on 2026-06-18** — *"Gemini Code Assist IDE extensions stopped serving requests for the Gemini Code Assist for individuals, Google AI Pro, and Google AI Ultra tiers … This also applies to usage of Gemini CLI … you can no longer use the Login with Google option"*, with consumer accounts directed to **the Antigravity family** and Standard/Enterprise explicitly unchanged. **Separately, the terms forbid nothing relevant:** policies.google.com/terms restricts only *"using automated means to access content … in violation of the machine-readable instructions on our web pages (for example, robots.txt files…)"* — conditional on robots.txt, **materially unlike §G.5's Anthropic clause** — and ai.google.dev/gemini-api/terms carries no clause against scripted or agent-driven use (its one relevant constraint is regional). **THE REASON IS AVAILABILITY, NOT TERMS, AND THAT IS UPSTREAM OF AND STRONGER THAN W37's sandbox EPERM.** **CONFLICT, recorded rather than resolved:** the gemini-cli README still advertises *"Sign in with Google"* with *"Free tier: 60 requests/min and 1,000 requests/day"*; two Google sources disagree, the deprecation page is the more specific and the more recent, and the README's freshness was not verified. **The live question now standing where v90's Gemini half stood is R43** |
| **R41** | Does a held `caffeinate -i` assertion, visible in `pmset -g assertions`, satisfy `night_capable`, and does it survive a lid close? | One measured cell on this Mac: hold the assertion, read `pmset -g assertions`, close the lid, read `run.started` against the wake | **O81's contract** and with it §20.2 row 7: if it does, the founder's act is a bounded per-night command rather than `pmset -a disablesleep 1`, a machine-wide policy change they deferred; if it does not, the deferral is what gates the first night *(added 2026-09-06: challenge D P1-3)* | **PARTIAL 2026-09-07 (close-M.md): visibility ANSWERED, the lid answered for `-i` and for `-s` on battery, BLOCKED for `-s` on AC — and the answer changes the founder's act rather than confirming it.** **The assertion IS readable** — `pid <N>(caffeinate): … PreventUserIdleSystemSleep named: "caffeinate command-line tool"`, appearing within 2 s and gone on exit. **But NEITHER HALF OF `pmset -g assertions` SUFFICES ALONE, and that is the finding:** at baseline with no caffeinate running the system-wide `PreventUserIdleSystemSleep` already reads **1** (held by `coreaudiod` and `powerd`'s *"Prevent sleep while display is on"*), and on battery a `caffeinate -s` was **listed per-process while system-wide `PreventSystemSleep` stayed 0** — the assertion existed and did not bind. **`night_capable` must read both, and treat listed-but-zero as FALSE (O81).** **A bounded `caffeinate` does NOT substitute for `pmset -a disablesleep 1`:** *"-i Create an assertion to prevent the system from idle sleeping"* and *"-s … valid only when system is running on AC power"* (CAFFEINATE(8), read on this machine), **the man page names no flag that speaks to the lid**, and two `Clamshell Sleep`s were measured inside a held `PreventSystemSleep` window — both on battery, consistent with the AC-only restriction and settling nothing about AC. **So §20.2 row 7 must NOT be closed as though a per-night command replaced the deferred machine-wide change.** **And R37 outranks both: the 6 h 41 min `Low Power Sleep` at 1% charge is a state no assertion of any kind prevents.** **The remaining cell is a PHYSICAL ACT and is BLOCKED ON THE FOUNDER:** hold `caffeinate -s` on AC, close the lid, wait, reopen, read whether a `Clamshell Sleep` was logged inside the window. Second residue, and it may make the whole half low-value: **whether a lid close is even the relevant risk for an unattended night** — the lid is presumably open on a desk |

only the outer one; and a replication whose first probe put the deny-listed path **in the prompt** had the
child **decline on its own reasoning**, a model refusal that would have been scored as a sandbox success and
**biased toward the reassuring answer**. **A probe that names what it is testing measures the model, not the
mechanism.** **Every lane was single-family, one agent, one pass, one machine, one day**, so this round meets
neither the `risk: high` two-family predicate nor the `irreversible` 2-of-3 requirement, and every reversal in
